莱蒂斯·金纳斯利

Lettice Kinnersley
作者： Heather Wolfe
摘要中文翻译莱蒂丝·金纳斯利(1573–1632)是理查德·巴戈特(1530–1597)和玛丽·桑德斯(生年不详)的小女儿。她有两个哥哥,沃尔特(1557–1622)和安东尼(1558年生),以及三个姐姐,玛格丽特·特鲁(1551–1587)、安·布劳顿(1555–1619)和多萝西·奥克弗(1562–1628)。1601年,28岁的她嫁给了弗朗西斯·金纳斯利。他们在1603年育有一子托马斯,或许还有另外两个男孩内德和迪克,这些名字出现在她丈夫给她哥哥的一封信中,信中提到他们有麻疹。弗朗西斯与他的父亲安东尼·金纳斯利关系疏远,莱蒂丝和弗朗西斯都开始依赖莱蒂丝的大哥沃尔特·巴戈特,沃尔特在1597年继承了父亲的遗产,作为他们的中间人和心腹。莱蒂丝的婚姻充满了财务和家庭困难。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在探索莱蒂斯·金纳斯利的历史背景和家庭关系,重点关注她的生活以及她在家庭结构中面临的挑战。背景简要概述了她的家庭血统和当时的社会动态。
设计与对象
这项研究是基于家庭记录和信件的历史叙述,考察了莱蒂斯·金纳斯利的生活、她的婚姻以及她家庭中的家庭关系。其中包括她于 1601 年与弗朗西斯·金纳斯利 (Francis Kinnersley) 结婚,并提到了他们的孩子,尤其是受 麻疹 影响的儿子。
核心方法
这些方法包括分析历史文献,包括家庭记录和信件,以重建莱蒂斯·金纳斯利的生活以及她的婚姻和家庭周围的环境。这些来源提供了有关她的血统、婚姻和孩子的健康状况的详细信息。
主要结果
主要结果包括莱蒂斯·金纳斯利 (Lettice Kinnersley) 于 1601 年与弗朗西斯·金纳斯利 (Francis Kinnersley) 结婚,他们的儿子托马斯 (Thomas) 于 1603 年出生,以及在她丈夫给哥哥的一封信中提到了另外两个儿子内德 (Ned) 和迪克 (Dick),这两个儿子的名字是 麻疹。
解释与创新
作者将这些发现解释为强调了莱蒂斯·金纳斯利面临的个人和家庭挑战,特别是在她的婚姻和影响孩子的健康问题的背景下。新颖之处在于对她一生的详细历史叙述以及她的儿子们对麻疹的具体提及。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果深入了解了麻疹的历史麻疹及其对家庭动态的影响,这可以为了解和管理历史背景下的传染病的公共卫生策略提供信息。
局限与证据强度
证据仅基于历史文献,可能不完整或有偏见。该研究的结果仅限于这些来源中提供的信息,并且由于缺乏直接的科学或医学数据,结果的可信度较低。

Lettice Kinnersley

Authors: Heather Wolfe
Original abstract (English)
Lettice Kinnersley (1573–1632) was the youngest daughter of Richard Bagot (1530–1597) and Mary Saunders (b. She had two older brothers, Walter (1557–1622) and Anthony (b. 1558), and three older sisters, Margaret Trew (1551–1587), Ann Broughton (1555–1619), and Dorothy Okeover (1562–1628). In 1601, at the age of 28, she married Francis Kinnersley. They had a son, Thomas, in 1603, and perhaps two other boys, Ned and Dick, who are mentioned as having measles in a letter from her husband to her brother. Francis was estranged from his father, Anthony Kinnersley, and both Lettice and Francis came to rely on Lettice’s oldest brother, Walter Bagot, who had inherited her father’s estate in 1597, as an intermediary and confidant. Lettice’s marriage was fraught with financial and familial difficulties.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aims to explore the historical context and familial relationships of Lettice Kinnersley, focusing on her life and the challenges she faced within her family structure. The background provides a brief overview of her family lineage and the social dynamics of the time.
Design and population
The study is a historical narrative based on family records and letters, examining Lettice Kinnersley's life, her marriage, and the familial relationships within her household. It includes her marriage to Francis Kinnersley in 1601 and the mention of their children, particularly the sons who were affected by measles.
Core methods
The methods involve analyzing historical documents, including family records and letters, to reconstruct Lettice Kinnersley's life and the circumstances surrounding her marriage and family. These sources provide details about her lineage, marriage, and the health status of her children.
Main results
The main results include Lettice Kinnersley's marriage to Francis Kinnersley in 1601, the birth of their son Thomas in 1603, and the mention of two other sons, Ned and Dick, who were noted to have measles in a letter from her husband to her brother.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret the findings as highlighting the personal and familial challenges Lettice Kinnersley faced, particularly in the context of her marriage and the health issues affecting her children. The novelty lies in the detailed historical account of her life and the specific mention of measles in her sons.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings provide insight into the historical prevalence of measles and its impact on family dynamics, which can inform public health strategies for understanding and managing infectious diseases in historical contexts.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence is based solely on historical documents, which may be incomplete or biased. The study's findings are limited to the information available in these sources, and the confidence in the results is moderate due to the lack of direct scientific or medical data.

麻疹 疫苗接种 赞比亚五个选定 暴发 地区的覆盖范围、护理人员的看法和信息来源:一项描述性横断面研究

Measles Vaccination Coverage, Caregiver Perceptions, and Information Sources in Five Selected Outbreak Districts of Zambia: A Descriptive Cross-Sectional Study
作者： Priscilla Nkonde Gardner, Dalitso Ngulube, Musole Chipoya, Kelvin Mwangilwa, Walter Azibadighi, Precious Kalubula, Lillian Lamba, Davie Simwaba, Roma Chilengi
摘要中文翻译摘要背景 麻疹 仍然是撒哈拉以南非洲地区的一个重大公共卫生问题,尽管赞比亚正在进行 麻疹 努力,但仍会定期经历 麻疹。了解 疫苗接种 覆盖范围、护理人员知识和信息来源的决定因素对于设计有效的免疫计划至关重要。本研究旨在评估赞比亚五个地区儿童的 麻疹 疫苗接种 覆盖范围、看护者的看法和信息来源。方法 对赞比亚五个地区(Chiengi、Kalabo、Kanchibiya、Mungwi 和 Mwinilunga)的 491 名 9 个月至 15 岁儿童进行了横断面研究。通过与护理人员的结构化访谈、疫苗接种 卡的审查以及家庭观察来收集数据。使用描述性统计来描述研究人群、疫苗接种 病史、麻疹 病例的临床表现、家庭环境因素和 疫苗接种 信息来源。摘要统计数据以频率、百分比、中位数和四分位数范围表示。结果 参与者的中位年龄为 6 岁(IQR:2.6 – 10.0),其中 57.6% 为女性。大多数(95.5%)居住在农村地区。疫苗接种 卡仅适用于 29.5% 的参与者。在持卡人中,97.2% 的持卡人至少记录过一次常规 麻疹 剂量,而 63.4% 的持卡人接受过两次常规剂量。看护者回忆表明,58.2% 的儿童在过去五年内参加过 麻疹 疫苗接种 活动。大多数护理人员 (93.5%) 认为 麻疹 是可以预防的。卫生工作者是 疫苗接种 信息的主要来源 (98.8%),其次是传统或宗教来源 (51.2%)。在 37 名有 麻疹 病史的儿童中,发烧和皮疹是最常见的症状(各占 97.3%)。10.6%的家庭住户过度拥挤,21.0%的家庭卫生条件差。结论 虽然有可用记录的儿童中的 疫苗接种 覆盖率似乎足够,但没有 疫苗接种 文件的儿童比例很高,这表明在监测免疫状况方面存在显着差距。卫生工作者仍然是 疫苗接种 信息最值得信赖和利用的来源。加强 疫苗接种 记录保存系统、提高社区参与度和利用多种信息渠道可以增强 麻疹 麻疹 的覆盖范围和赞比亚的 麻疹 准备情况。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本研究重点关注赞比亚五个 麻疹 区(Chiengi、Kalabo、Kanchibiya、Mungwi 和 Mwinilunga)9 个月至 15 岁儿童的 麻疹 疫苗接种 覆盖范围、看护者认知和信息来源。核心问题是评估影响 疫苗接种 状况和知识的因素,为免疫规划提供信息。
证据基础与方法
对 491 名儿童进行了一项描述性横断面研究。通过与护理人员的结构化访谈、疫苗接种 卡的审查以及家庭观察来收集数据。描述性统计数据,包括频率、百分比、中位数和四分位数范围,用于分析数据。
共识与结论
在持有 疫苗接种 卡的儿童中,97.2% 的儿童至少服用过一剂常规 麻疹 剂量,63.4% 的儿童服用过两剂。回忆显示,58.2% 的看护者在过去五年中参与了 麻疹 疫苗接种 活动。大多数护理人员 (93.5%) 认为 麻疹 是可以预防的,卫生工作者是主要信息来源 (98.8%)。
争议与缺口
存在显着差距,因为只有 29.5% 的参与者可以获得 疫苗接种 卡,这阻碍了免疫状态的准确监测。观察到家庭过度拥挤(10.6%)和卫生条件差(21.0%),但没有探讨它们对 麻疹 传播 的直接影响。
科研与实践启示
加强 疫苗接种 记录保存系统、提高社区参与度以及利用多种信息渠道(包括 51.2% 的传统或宗教来源)可以增强 麻疹 麻疹 的覆盖范围和 暴发 在赞比亚的准备情况。

Measles Vaccination Coverage, Caregiver Perceptions, and Information Sources in Five Selected Outbreak Districts of Zambia: A Descriptive Cross-Sectional Study

Authors: Priscilla Nkonde Gardner, Dalitso Ngulube, Musole Chipoya, Kelvin Mwangilwa, Walter Azibadighi, Precious Kalubula, Lillian Lamba, Davie Simwaba, Roma Chilengi
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Measles remains a significant public health concern in sub-Saharan Africa, with Zambia experiencing periodic outbreaks despite ongoing vaccination efforts. Understanding the determinants of vaccination coverage, caregiver knowledge, and information sources is essential for designing effective immunization programs. This study aimed to assess measles vaccination coverage, caregiver perceptions, and information sources among children in five districts of Zambia. Methods A cross-sectional study was conducted among 491 children aged 9 months to 15 years across five districts in Zambia (Chiengi, Kalabo, Kanchibiya, Mungwi, and Mwinilunga). Data were collected through structured interviews with caregivers, review of vaccination cards, and household observations. Descriptive statistics were used to characterize the study population, vaccination history, clinical presentation of measles cases, household environmental factors, and sources of vaccination information. Summary statistics are presented as frequencies, percentages, medians, and interquartile ranges. Results The median age of participants was 6 years (IQR: 2.6 − 10.0), with 57.6% being female. The majority (95.5%) resided in rural areas. Vaccination cards were available for only 29.5% of participants. Among cardholders, 97.2% had documented at least one routine measles dose, while 63.4% had received two routine doses. Caregiver recall indicated that 58.2% of children had participated in a measles vaccination campaign within the previous five years. Most caregivers (93.5%) believed measles is preventable. Health workers were the predominant source of vaccination information (98.8%), followed by traditional or religious sources (51.2%). Among 37 children with a history of prior measles illness, fever and rash were the most commonly reported symptoms (97.3% each). Household overcrowding was observed in 10.6% of households, and poor sanitation in 21.0%. Conclusions While vaccination coverage among children with available records appears adequate, the high proportion of children without vaccination documentation represents a significant gap in monitoring immunization status. Health workers remain the most trusted and utilized source of vaccination information. Strengthening vaccination record-keeping systems, improving community engagement, and leveraging multiple information channels could enhance measles vaccination coverage and outbreak preparedness in Zambia.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This study focused on measles vaccination coverage, caregiver perceptions, and information sources among children aged 9 months to 15 years in five outbreak districts of Zambia (Chiengi, Kalabo, Kanchibiya, Mungwi, and Mwinilunga). The central question was to assess factors influencing vaccination status and knowledge to inform immunization programs.
Evidence base and method
A descriptive cross-sectional study was conducted with 491 children. Data were collected through structured interviews with caregivers, review of vaccination cards, and household observations. Descriptive statistics, including frequencies, percentages, medians, and interquartile ranges, were used to analyze the data.
Consensus and conclusions
Among children with vaccination cards, 97.2% had at least one routine measles dose, and 63.4% had two doses. Caregiver recall indicated 58.2% participation in a measles vaccination campaign in the previous five years. Most caregivers (93.5%) believed measles is preventable, and health workers were the primary information source (98.8%).
Controversies and gaps
A significant gap exists as vaccination cards were available for only 29.5% of participants, hindering accurate monitoring of immunization status. Household overcrowding (10.6%) and poor sanitation (21.0%) were observed, but their direct impact on measles transmission was not explored.
Research and practice implications
Strengthening vaccination record-keeping systems, improving community engagement, and leveraging multiple information channels (including traditional or religious sources at 51.2%) could enhance measles vaccination coverage and outbreak preparedness in Zambia.

2019-2024 年加纳切雷波尼区 麻疹 监测 系统评估

Evaluation of the measles surveillance system in Chereponi District, Ghana, 2019-2024
作者： Richard Dzeha, Owusu Amponsah
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
使用 CDC 更新的公共卫生 监测 系统评估指南,对 2019 年至 2024 年加纳切雷波尼区 麻疹 监测 系统的设计、实施和性能进行评估。
证据基础与方法
对所有 26 个卫生机构和 13 个有意选择的关键信息提供者进行了横断面混合方法 监测 系统评估。定量数据是通过设施评估和 SORMAS 记录收集的,而定性数据是通过半结构化访谈获得的。进行了描述性统计、PVP、Cochran-Armitage 趋势分析和主题分析。
共识与结论
麻疹 监测 系统正常运行,支持加纳 IDSR 框架内的及时病例检测、实验室确认和 暴发 响应。及时性(100%)、数据质量(96.2%)和代表性(88.5%)是最强的属性,而有用性(42.3%)和稳定性(38.5%)是最弱的。实验室确认率从 0%(2019-2021 年)大幅上升至 2024 年的 30.8%。
争议与缺口
运营弱点包括进修培训不足、人员流失、交通限制、实验室反馈延迟、互联网和电力不可靠以及基于社区的报告不一致。这些因素导致实用性和稳定性得分较低,表明劳动力能力、物流、数字基础设施和基于社区的 监测 方面存在差距。
科研与实践启示
加强劳动力能力、物流、实验室反馈、数字基础设施和基于社区的 麻疹 对于提高系统稳定性和推进 麻疹 消除工作至关重要。

Evaluation of the measles surveillance system in Chereponi District, Ghana, 2019-2024

Authors: Richard Dzeha, Owusu Amponsah
Original abstract (English)
Objectives To evaluate the design, implementation, and performance of the measles surveillance system in Chereponi District, Ghana, from 2019 to 2024 using the United States Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) Updated Guidelines for Evaluating Public Health Surveillance Systems. Study design Cross-sectional mixed-methods surveillance system evaluation. Methods A mixed-methods evaluation was conducted across all 26 health facilities implementing measles surveillance and 13 purposively selected key informants. Quantitative data were collected through facility assessments and review of Surveillance Outbreak Response Management and Analysis System (SORMAS) records. Qualitative data were obtained through semi-structured interviews. Descriptive statistics, predictive value positive (PVP), Cochran–Armitage trend analysis, and thematic analysis were performed. Results Timeliness was the strongest surveillance attribute (100%), followed by data quality (96.2%) and representativeness (88.5%). Acceptability (80.8%), simplicity (73.1%), and flexibility (61.5%) showed moderate performance, whereas usefulness (42.3%) and stability (38.5%) were weakest. During 2019-2024, 167 suspected measles cases were reported, of which 31 were laboratory confirmed (PVP = 18.6%; 95% CI: 13.4-25.3%). Laboratory confirmation rates increased significantly from 0% during 2019-2021 to 30.8% in 2024 (Cochran-Armitage χ 2 = 14.57, P < 0.001). Qualitative findings attributed operational weaknesses to inadequate refresher training, staff attrition, transport constraints, delayed laboratory feedback, unreliable internet and electricity, and inconsistent community-based reporting. Conclusions The measles surveillance system was functional and supported timely case detection, laboratory confirmation, and outbreak response within Ghana's IDSR framework. However, strengthening workforce capacity, logistics, laboratory feedback, digital infrastructure, and community-based surveillance is essential to improve system stability and advance measles elimination effort.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Evaluation of the design, implementation, and performance of the measles surveillance system in Chereponi District, Ghana, from 2019 to 2024 using the CDC Updated Guidelines for Evaluating Public Health Surveillance Systems.
Evidence base and method
Cross-sectional mixed-methods surveillance system evaluation conducted across all 26 health facilities and 13 purposively selected key informants. Quantitative data were collected through facility assessments and SORMAS records, while qualitative data were obtained through semi-structured interviews. Descriptive statistics, PVP, Cochran–Armitage trend analysis, and thematic analysis were performed.
Consensus and conclusions
The measles surveillance system was functional and supported timely case detection, laboratory confirmation, and outbreak response within Ghana's IDSR framework. Timeliness (100%), data quality (96.2%), and representativeness (88.5%) were the strongest attributes, while usefulness (42.3%) and stability (38.5%) were weakest. Laboratory confirmation rates increased significantly from 0% (2019-2021) to 30.8% in 2024.
Controversies and gaps
Operational weaknesses included inadequate refresher training, staff attrition, transport constraints, delayed laboratory feedback, unreliable internet and electricity, and inconsistent community-based reporting. These factors contributed to low usefulness and stability scores, indicating gaps in workforce capacity, logistics, digital infrastructure, and community-based surveillance.
Research and practice implications
Strengthening workforce capacity, logistics, laboratory feedback, digital infrastructure, and community-based surveillance is essential to improve system stability and advance measles elimination efforts.

变化世界中的儿科 疫苗接种。对儿童健康的影响、疫苗 犹豫以及免疫接种的未来前景

Pediatric Vaccination in a Changing World. Impact on Child Health, Vaccine Hesitancy, and Future Perspectives of Immunization
作者： Ferizat Dika – Haxhirexha, Ledia Qatipi, Blinera Ballanca, Sevdije Koxha, Shqiponja Turkeshi, Afërdita Ademi, Ferizate Dika-Haxhirexha
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注儿科 疫苗接种,特别审查其对儿童健康的影响、疫苗 的犹豫以及免疫接种的未来前景。核心问题涉及综合关于儿科 疫苗接种 的健康和社会影响的证据、疫苗 犹豫的决定因素以及免疫计划的未来方向。
证据基础与方法
进行了叙述性审查,整合了流行病学数据、模型研究以及世界卫生组织、联合国儿童基金会的最新报告和同行评审文献。该综述研究了覆盖率、疾病复发和 疫苗 信心的行为决定因素的趋势。
共识与结论
Routine immunization has averted an estimated 154 million deaths since 1974, predominantly in children under five. However, in 2024, 14.3 million infants received no vaccine doses, and measles re-emerged in multiple countries, threatening elimination status. Vaccine hesitancy was strongly influenced by misinformation, sociocultural factors, and trust in health systems. Resilient health infrastructure, equitable access, and credible communication by frontline providers are essential to sustain progress.
争议与缺口
审查强调,仅凭科学进步并不能确保免疫收益。证据差距包括需要增强公众信任、解决行为决定因素以及确保公平交付 疫苗,以克服错误信息和获取不平等等挑战。
科研与实践启示
加强公众信任、解决行为决定因素并确保公平交付对于儿科 疫苗接种 的未来至关重要。未来的研究和实践应侧重于改进沟通策略、增强卫生系统的弹性以及解决 疫苗 获取障碍。

Pediatric Vaccination in a Changing World. Impact on Child Health, Vaccine Hesitancy, and Future Perspectives of Immunization

Authors: Ferizat Dika – Haxhirexha, Ledia Qatipi, Blinera Ballanca, Sevdije Koxha, Shqiponja Turkeshi, Afërdita Ademi, Ferizate Dika-Haxhirexha
Original abstract (English)
Introduction: Vaccination remains one of the most effective and cost‐efficient public health interventions, dramatically reducing childhood morbidity and mortality from vaccine‐preventable diseases. However, the global immunization landscape has recently been challenged by pandemic disruptions, misinformation, inequities in access, and resurgent outbreaks. The Aim is to synthesize current evidence on the health and societal impact of pediatric vaccination, examine determinants of vaccine hesitancy, and highlight future perspectives for immunization programs. Methods; A narrative review was conducted, integrating epidemiological data, modelling studies, and recent reports from WHO, UNICEF, and peer‐reviewed literature. Particular attention was given to trends in coverage, disease resurgence, and behavioral determinants of vaccine confidence. Results: Routine immunization has averted an estimated 154 million deaths since 1974, predominantly in children under five. Yet, in 2024, 14.3 million infants received no vaccine doses, and measles re‐emerged in multiple countries, threatening elimination status. Vaccine hesitancy was strongly influenced by misinformation, sociocultural factors, and trust in health systems. Evidence underscores that resilient health infrastructure, equitable access, and credible communication by frontline providers are essential to sustain progress. Conclusions: Scientific advances alone cannot secure immunization gains. Strengthening public trust, addressing behavioral determinants, and ensuring equitable delivery are critical for the future of pediatric vaccination.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on pediatric vaccination, specifically examining its impact on child health, vaccine hesitancy, and future perspectives of immunization. The central question involves synthesizing evidence on the health and societal impact of pediatric vaccination, determinants of vaccine hesitancy, and future directions for immunization programs.
Evidence base and method
A narrative review was conducted, integrating epidemiological data, modeling studies, and recent reports from WHO, UNICEF, and peer-reviewed literature. The review examined trends in coverage, disease resurgence, and behavioral determinants of vaccine confidence.
Consensus and conclusions
Routine immunization has averted an estimated 154 million deaths since 1974, predominantly in children under five. However, in 2024, 14.3 million infants received no vaccine doses, and measles re-emerged in multiple countries, threatening elimination status. Vaccine hesitancy was strongly influenced by misinformation, sociocultural factors, and trust in health systems. Resilient health infrastructure, equitable access, and credible communication by frontline providers are essential to sustain progress.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights that scientific advances alone cannot secure immunization gains. Evidence gaps include the need for stronger public trust, addressing behavioral determinants, and ensuring equitable delivery of vaccines to overcome challenges like misinformation and inequities in access.
Research and practice implications
Strengthening public trust, addressing behavioral determinants, and ensuring equitable delivery are critical for the future of pediatric vaccination. Future research and practice should focus on improving communication strategies, enhancing health system resilience, and addressing barriers to vaccine access.

加强外展活动对巴基斯坦免疫覆盖率的影响:二次数据分析的证据(2011-2021 年)

Impact of Enhanced Outreach Activities on Immunization Coverage in Pakistan: Evidence from Secondary Data Analysis (2011–2021)
作者： Shah Nawaz Jiskani, Musa Khan, Osama Mere, Wei Xia, Atta Ur Rehman, Mohammed Osama Mere
摘要中文翻译背景:尽管扩大免疫计划(EPI)在提高全球 疫苗接种 覆盖率方面取得了显着进展,但在一些中低收入国家,疫苗 的吸收和可及性仍然存在不平等。巴基斯坦的常规 疫苗接种 吸收率在联邦地理区域之间差异很大,并且持续低于获得最佳群体免疫力所需的标准阈值。联邦免疫局 (FDI) 与省级免疫扩大计划合作实施了强化外展活动 (EOA),以促进选定地区的免疫接种服务,以解决边缘化和难以到达的社区的免疫覆盖率差距。这项二手数据研究的目的是评估 EOA 是否提高了该国的免疫覆盖率。方法:对联邦免疫局 2011-2021 年地区月行政数据的纵向面板分析进行了分析。该研究调查审查了 158 个地区的多种抗原的每月 疫苗接种 记录,涵盖了特定月份成功开展 EOA 的地区。进行了国家和国家以下各级的分析,以衡量加强外展活动 (EOAs) 影响的地理差异。使用配对 t 检验比较 EOA 和非 EOA 月份的平均 疫苗接种 覆盖率。结果:对 19,068 个月地区观察结果的分析中纳入了总计 2430 个月的 EOA。国家级分析显示,所有抗原在 EOA 月份期间的平均 麻疹 均较高,但仅在 IPV 和 麻疹 第二剂中观察到统计学上显着的增加 (p < 0.05)。省级结果表明,服务欠缺地区的影响更为明显。开伯尔-普赫图赫瓦省、俾路支省、旁遮普省和信德省表现出大多数抗原的 疫苗接种 计数在统计学上显着增加。由于基线人口覆盖率以及医疗保健部门能力的差异,各地区的改善程度有所不同。结论:在巴基斯坦,通过加强外展活动,常规免疫计数得到了改善,特别是在初始 疫苗接种 率较低且服务可及性有限的省份。加强外展战略,加上一致的固定地点免疫服务,可能会加速实现公平免疫覆盖率的进展,并有助于实现《2030 年免疫议程》目标。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该研究的重点是评估加强外展活动 (EOAs) 对巴基斯坦免疫覆盖率的影响,特别是针对 麻疹病毒 和相关实体。核心问题是,这些外展活动是否提高了边缘化和难以接触社区的 麻疹 率,尤其是 麻疹 和其他抗原。
证据基础与方法
证据基础由联邦免疫局 (FDI) 2011-2021 年的纵向面板数据组成。审查方法包括分析 158 个地区的地区月行政数据,使用配对 t 检验比较 EOA 月份和非 EOA 月份的 疫苗接种 覆盖率。进行了国家和地方分析,以评估EOA影响的地理差异。
共识与结论
研究发现,国家级分析显示,所有抗原在 EOA 月份期间的平均 麻疹 均较高,麻疹 第二剂和 IPV 的增加具有统计学意义(p < 0.05)。省级结果表明,这种影响在服务欠缺的地区更为明显,其中开伯尔-普赫图赫瓦省、俾路支省、旁遮普省和信德省的大多数抗原的 疫苗接种 计数显示出显着改善。结论是,加强外展活动提高了常规免疫覆盖率,特别是在初始 疫苗接种 率较低且服务可及性有限的省份。
争议与缺口
该研究承认基线人口覆盖率和医疗保健部门能力存在差异,这可能解释了不同地区改善程度不同的原因。然而,它没有提供影响这些差异的具体机制或背景因素的详细信息。此外,该研究并未解决观察期之后 EOA 的长期可持续性或有效性。
科研与实践启示
研究结果表明,加强外展战略与固定地点免疫服务相结合,可以加速实现公平免疫覆盖的进展。该研究强调了在服务欠缺地区采取有针对性的干预措施的必要性,并强调了一致实施 EOA 以实现 2030 年免疫议程目标的重要性。

Impact of Enhanced Outreach Activities on Immunization Coverage in Pakistan: Evidence from Secondary Data Analysis (2011–2021)

Authors: Shah Nawaz Jiskani, Musa Khan, Osama Mere, Wei Xia, Atta Ur Rehman, Mohammed Osama Mere
Original abstract (English)
Background: Despite noteworthy progress in the Expanded Program on Immunization (EPI) for improving vaccination coverage worldwide, inequalities in vaccine uptake and accessibility persist in several middle- and low-income states. Pakistan’s routine vaccination uptake varies greatly between federal geographical regions and continues to fall below the standard threshold needed to attain optimal herd immunity. The Federal Directorate of Immunization (FDI) in collaboration with the provincial Expanded Program on Immunizations implemented Enhanced Outreach Activities (EOAs) to boost immunization delivery in selected districts in order to address immunization coverage gaps in marginalized and difficult-to-reach communities. The purpose of this secondary data research was to evaluate whether EOAs improved immunization coverage in the country. Methods: The Federal Directorate of Immunization’s longitudinal panel analysis of district–month administrative data for the years 2011–2021 have been analyzed. The research investigation reviewed 158 districts’ monthly vaccination records for multiple antigens, covering regions where EOAs were successfully carried out in particular months. National and subnational analyses were carried out to measure geographical variations in the impact of Enhanced Outreach Activities (EOAs). The average vaccination coverage during EOAs and non-EOAs months was compared using the paired t-test. Results: An aggregate of 2430 months with EOAs were included in the analysis of 19,068 monthly district observations. National level analysis revealed higher average vaccinations during the EOA months for all antigens, but a statistically significant increase was observed for IPV and measles second dose (p < 0.05) only. Provincial-level results indicate that the impact was more evident in underserved regions. Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Balochistan, Punjab, and Sindh demonstrated statistically significant increases in vaccination counts for most antigens. Due to variations in baseline population coverage, as well as health care sector capability, the extent of improvements differed throughout regions. Conclusions: In Pakistan, routine immunization counts has been improved via enhanced outreach initiatives, especially in provinces with lower initial vaccination rates and restricted accessibility to services. Strengthening outreach strategies, together with consistent fixed-site immunization services, is likely to accelerate progress toward equitable immunization coverage and contribute to achieving the Immunization Agenda 2030 goals.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study focuses on evaluating the impact of Enhanced Outreach Activities (EOAs) on immunization coverage in Pakistan, particularly targeting measles virus and related entities. The central question is whether these outreach initiatives improved vaccination rates, especially for measles and other antigens, in marginalized and difficult-to-reach communities.
Evidence base and method
The evidence base consists of longitudinal panel data from the Federal Directorate of Immunization (FDI) covering 2011–2021. The review method involved analyzing district–month administrative data for 158 districts, comparing vaccination coverage during EOA months with non-EOA months using a paired t-test. National and subnational analyses were conducted to assess geographical variations in the impact of EOAs.
Consensus and conclusions
The study found that national-level analysis revealed higher average vaccinations during EOA months for all antigens, with statistically significant increases for measles second dose and IPV (p < 0.05). Provincial-level results indicated that the impact was more pronounced in underserved regions, with Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Balochistan, Punjab, and Sindh showing significant improvements in vaccination counts for most antigens. The conclusion is that enhanced outreach initiatives have improved routine immunization coverage, particularly in provinces with lower initial vaccination rates and restricted service accessibility.
Controversies and gaps
The study acknowledges variations in baseline population coverage and healthcare sector capability, which may explain differing extents of improvement across regions. However, it does not provide detailed information on the specific mechanisms or contextual factors influencing these disparities. Additionally, the study does not address the long-term sustainability or effectiveness of EOAs beyond the observed period.
Research and practice implications
The findings suggest that strengthening outreach strategies, in conjunction with fixed-site immunization services, could accelerate progress toward equitable immunization coverage. The study highlights the need for targeted interventions in underserved regions and emphasizes the importance of consistent implementation of EOAs to achieve the Immunization Agenda 2030 goals.

环境 麻疹 作为 麻疹 检测工具:与巴基斯坦临床病例的相关性

Environmental Surveillance as a Tool for Measles Detection: Correlation with Clinical Cases in Pakistan
作者： Wardah Mujahid, Sonia Qureshi, Junaid Iqbal, Muhammad Jawwad, Mohammad Tahir Yousafzai, Furqan Kabir, Suleman Rana, Masroor Alam, Muhammad Usman, Muhammad Anas

Environmental Surveillance as a Tool for Measles Detection: Correlation with Clinical Cases in Pakistan

Authors: Wardah Mujahid, Sonia Qureshi, Junaid Iqbal, Muhammad Jawwad, Mohammad Tahir Yousafzai, Furqan Kabir, Suleman Rana, Masroor Alam, Muhammad Usman, Muhammad Anas

Delaware Declares Measles Outbreak - State of Delaware News (.gov)
新闻简报特拉华州已正式宣布 麻疹 暴发,标志着重大公共卫生问题。虽然官方公告中没有披露具体病例数和受影响人群,但声明强调了情况的严重性。特拉华州公共卫生部 (DPH) 此前于 2026 年 7 月 20 日确认了十多年来首例 麻疹 阳性病例,这可能促使发布了 麻疹 声明。公共卫生官员预计将实施应对措施,包括接触者追踪、疫苗接种 活动和公共咨询,以遏制传播。麻疹 声明旨在提醒医疗保健提供者和公众对 麻疹 的潜在暴露和症状保持警惕。

A high priorityPublic-health news

Delaware Declares Measles Outbreak - State of Delaware News (.gov)

News Brief
The State of Delaware has officially declared a measles outbreak, marking a significant public health concern. While the specific number of cases and affected populations have not been disclosed in the official announcement, the declaration underscores the seriousness of the situation. The Delaware Department of Public Health (DPH) had previously confirmed the first positive measles case in over a decade on July 20, 2026, which likely prompted the outbreak declaration. Public health officials are expected to implement response measures including contact tracing, vaccination campaigns, and public advisories to contain the spread. The outbreak declaration serves as an alert to healthcare providers and the public to remain vigilant about potential exposures and symptoms of measles.

Measles outbreak at AZ ICE facility prompts quarantine - FOX 10 Phoenix

News Brief
Maricopa County health officials are responding to a significant measles outbreak with 25 confirmed cases in 2026, a dramatic increase from the historical average of one case per year. An out-of-state visitor tested positive for measles, exposing the public at six locations in Phoenix and Chandler between July 12-13, including Chase Field and Chandler Fashion Center. The highly contagious virus can survive in the air for up to two hours, posing a 90% infection risk to unvaccinated individuals. Health officials are urging residents to verify MMR vaccination status and monitor for symptoms, which include high fever, rash, and respiratory issues. In response, the county is recommending early vaccination for infants and implementing quarantine measures at an ICE facility where seven detainees tested positive.

Maricopa County Measles Alert: Infected tourist exposes Chase Field, Chandler Fashion Center, 4 other Valley spots By Elias Weiss FOX 10 Phoenix Health Published July 21, 2026 4:34 PM MST Share Copy Link Email Facebook Twitter LinkedIn Reddit Published July 21, 2026 4:34 PM MST article The measles virus, paramyxoviridae from the Morbillivirus family, transmission microscopy view. (Photo by: BSIP/Universal Images Group via Getty Images) The Brief Maricopa County health officials have identified six new public measles exposure sites in Phoenix and Chandler after an out-of-state visitor tested positive for the virus. The new cases bring the county's total to 25 confirmed measles cases in 2026, a dramatic increase compared to the historical average of just one case per year. Anyone who visited the exposed locations between July 12 and July 13 should verify their MMR vaccination status and watch for symptoms like a high fever and rash. PHOENIX - A visitor to Maricopa County has tested positive for measles, exposing the public to the highly contagious virus at six new locations across Phoenix and Chandler, health officials announced on Tuesday. What we know: The exposures occurred between July 12 and July 13 at popular venues including Chase Field, Chandler Fashion Center and several local restaurants. This brings the county's total to 25 confirmed cases in 2026, marking a significant spike compared to the historical average of just one case per year over the last three decades. The Maricopa County Department of Public Health is urging residents and visitors who were at the exposed locations to check their immunization records and immediately begin monitoring for symptoms. The measles virus is extremely infectious and can survive in the air for up to two hours after an infected person has left the area, meaning anyone passing through the same space could be at risk. Unvaccinated people have a 90% chance of contracting the virus if exposed. Featured article Measles: Multiple new cases reported in Maricopa County Maricopa County Health reports 7 new cases of measles, making the total 21 cases for the year. Health officials say these cases are above average. Timeline: The newly identified exposure trail began on Sunday, July 12, at The Taco Spot on 54th Street in Chandler during the mid-afternoon, followed by an overnight stay at the nearby Sonesta Select Hotel that lasted until Monday afternoon. On Sunday evening, the infected person also spent five hours at Chase Field in downtown Phoenix, from 7 p.m. to midnight. The potential exposures continued into the early hours of Monday morning at the IHOP on Ray Road in Phoenix, followed by late morning and afternoon visits to bb.q Chicken on Chandler Boulevard and the Chandler Fashion Center. Previous exposures were also identified earlier in July at Tempe Marketplace and a Surprise Walmart. Dig deeper: Health officials warn that symptoms typically appear within seven to 12 days after exposure but can take up to three weeks to surface. The telltale signs include a high fever over 101 degrees, red and watery eyes, a persistent cough and a runny nose. These are usually followed by a red, raised and blotchy rash that begins at the hairline on the face and spreads downward across the body. Measles outbreak at AZ ICE facility prompts quarantine A measles outbreak at a Florence ICE facility prompted a quarantine after federal officials said seven detainees tested positive for the virus. The outbreak halted movement in and out of the facility, and even delayed some deportation flights. What they're saying: Dr. Ariella Dale, Maricopa County's chief science officer, emphasized the effectiveness of the measles, mumps and rubella vaccine. "The continuing identification of new measles cases and exposures highlights how easily this virus spreads," she said. "We have an effective, trusted vaccine that has kept the virus from spreading like this for decades. As a community, we can use that same tool to stop spread now." What you can do: Because there is no specific medical treatment for measles and the disease carries risks of severe, long-term brain complications, the health department is taking extra precautions. In alignment with CDC guidance, they are currently recommending an early vaccine dose for infants aged six to 11 months. Anyone who develops symptoms is instructed to stay home and call a healthcare provider before seeking in-person care to prevent further spread of the disease. The Source: Information in this report was gathered from the Maricopa County Department of Public Health. HealthPhoenix

B routine priorityPublic-health news

What to know about measles following Delaware outbreak

News Brief
Delaware health officials have declared a measles outbreak following four confirmed cases in Kent County, all involving unvaccinated adult men. This outbreak meets the CDC's definition of three or more connected cases. Nationwide, measles cases have surged to 2,295 so far in 2026, the highest since 1991. The virus poses a significant risk to unvaccinated individuals, pregnant women, the immunocompromised, and infants. Health officials are conducting extensive contact tracing and urging exposed individuals to seek guidance. While there is no specific antiviral treatment, the MMR vaccine can be administered post-exposure for those not immune. Delaware residents are advised to check their vaccination status and consult healthcare providers for concerns.
Original source text (English)
HEALTH Robert F. Kennedy Jr. What to know about measles following Delaware outbreak Isabel Hughes Delaware News Journal Updated July 24, 2026, 1:46 p.m. ET Earlier this week, Delaware health officials declared a measles outbreak following four confirmed cases in the state. All patients were adult men in Kent County, the Delaware Division of Public Health said. Per the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, a measles outbreak is defined as three or more cases connected by time and location. While Delaware officials could not provide much detail about the cases due to health privacy restrictions, the state's outbreak declaration suggests the cases are related. With cases on the rise nationwide and outbreaks in nearby states, including Pennsylvania, here's what Delaware residents should know. Who has tested positive for measles in Delaware? The Division of Public Health said all four people who tested positive as of July 22 are unvaccinated adult men in Kent County. The health department cannot give more information "due to medical privacy restrictions." Who is most at risk during a measles outbreak? People who are unvaccinated, pregnant, immunocompromised or too young to be vaccinated face the highest risk of severe complications, DPH said. The first dose for infants is usually given between 12 and 15 months. Anyone who cannot get the vaccine but who has come into contact with someone suspected to have measles should immediately contact their primary care provider or Delaware's Office of Infectious Disease Epidemiology at (302) 744-4990 for guidance. How do I know if I've been exposed? In announcing its first confirmed case in more than a decade on July 20, Delaware health officials said "extensive" contact tracing was ongoing to identify anyone exposed to the person who tested positive. "DPH will notify people who were identified as exposed to confirm whether they have been vaccinated against measles, provide educational resources, and to recommend treatment when necessary." Given the Delaware measles outbreak, can my infant get an early dose of the MMR vaccine? Speak to your pediatrician. Babies six months and older are physically able to receive the measles, mumps and rubella (MMR) vaccine , though an early dose − defined as before 12 months of age − won't count toward their regular vaccine schedule, which is typically two doses. The first dose is usually given between 12 and 15 months, while the second is typically given between 4 and 6 years of age. Are adults who were vaccinated decades ago still protected? If you were born before 1957, you are considered immune. If you were fully vaccinated, you’re also considered immune. If you’re concerned, however, your can check your vaccination status online at delvaxpublicportal.dhss.delaware.gov . If you were not vaccinated in Delaware or you can't find information, your doctor can order a blood test to check for immunity. Such testing is fairly common. What are measles? Measles is a highly contagious, acute viral illness that begins with early symptoms of fever, cough, runny nose and conjunctivitis (pink eye), lasting two to four days before rash onset. The rash typically occurs three to five days after symptoms begin and usually appears on the face and spreads down the body. Measles can cause severe health complications, including pneumonia, brain inflammation and death. The virus is transmitted by direct contact with infectious droplets or by airborne spread when an infected person breathes, coughs or sneezes. The measles virus can remain infectious in the air and on surfaces for up to two hours after an infected person leaves an area. Infected people are contagious from four days before the rash starts through four days after rash development. The incubation period for measles from exposure to fever is usually about 10 days, with a range of seven to 12 days. And from exposure to rash onset is usually about 14 days with a seven to 21-day range. How do you treat measles? There is no specific antiviral cure for the measles . However, if you haven't been previously vaccinated against the measles , you can still be vaccinated to head off the worst symptoms, if you act fast enough. "If people older than six months of age are exposed to measles and are not immune by previous illness or vaccination, they can get a measles vaccine for up to three days after contact with the virus," advised the Mayo Clinic . "After the vaccine you don't need to stay home, also called quarantine." Antibodies can also be given for up to six days after exposure, though they are prioritized for people who cannot get the measles vaccine, such as infants younger than 6 months old, pregnant women, severely immunocompromised people or people who have other medical contraindications to vaccination, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. How many cases of measles have been reported nationwide? New data released July 22 by Johns Hopkins University shows 2,295 measles cases have been reported nationwide so far this year, surpassing the 2,286 infections recorded during all of 2025 and marking the highest annual total since 1991. Why are measles on the rise in the United States? The United States declared measles eliminated in 2000 after recording just 86 cases that year, according to the CDC. But a series of outbreaks that began in early 2025, centered initially in Gaines County, Texas, has helped drive the virus' resurgence. Vaccination for measles has declined in recent years , resulting in a growing number of states no longer reporting rates consistent with herd immunity. Herd immunity occurs when more than 95% of people in a community are vaccinated and most people are protected, even if they cannot get the vaccine themselves, because those around them are immune and cannot spread the disease. Declining protections have also been attributed to the growing anti-vaccine movement, which was bolstered by the confirmation of vaccine skeptic Robert F. Kennedy Jr . as health secretary. In addition to making anti-vaccine comments, Kennedy also downplayed pediatric deaths related to measles and touted false cures and preventives, such as the use of vitamin A . During the height of the Texas outbreak, Kennedy encouraged inoculation against measles, pulling back from his previous anti-vaccine messaging. USA TODAY contributed to this report. Got a story tip or idea? Send to Isabel Hughes at ihughes@delawareonline.com. Featured Weekly Ad

B routine priorityPublic-health news

US sees highest number of measles cases in 35 years - BBC

News Brief
The US has reached a 35-year high in measles cases with 2,318 confirmed infections in 2026, surpassing last year's record of 2,289. The outbreak, linked to rising vaccine hesitancy, has spread across 45 states, with fatalities reported among unvaccinated individuals. Health officials warn the country may lose its measles elimination status by November, a status already lost by Canada. The highly contagious virus, which can cause fatal complications and long-term health issues, has prompted criticism of US vaccine policy changes. Despite the MMR vaccine being 97% effective, vaccination rates have dropped below 95% in some areas, threatening herd immunity. Ongoing outbreaks in multiple states underscore the urgent need for improved vaccination efforts.
Original source text (English)
US measles cases reach 35-year high 3 hours ago Madeline Halpert Getty Images The US has hit a 35-year high in measles cases, according to new figures from the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC). The US has seen 2,318 confirmed cases of measles in 2026, just seven months into the year, up from last year's high of 2,289, when two unvaccinated children died of the disease. The rising number of cases have come as vaccine hesitancy in the US has increased in recent years, leading to lower immunisation rates. In November, a panel is set to decide whether the US will lose its status of having eliminated measles, which Canada lost last year. A country loses elimination status after a year or more of continuous transmission of the same strain of the measles virus. The US has recorded more measles cases over the past 18 months than the total number over 25 years before that. In this US county, measles starts to feel like next pandemic The measles virus - with its signature blotchy red rash - used to infect millions of children a year before a vaccine was invented. The extremely contagious virus can be fatal, and some patients can experience long-term complications including encephalitis and immune amnesia, which resets the immune system, leaving it with only limited ability to respond to new infections. Last year, an outbreak among the Mennonite community in Texas killed two unvaccinated children, and an unvaccinated adult in neighbouring New Mexico. Over the past month, Virginia and Pennsylvania have been dealing with new measles outbreaks, while the state of Utah is still responding to a cluster of infections that began last year. Some 45 states have recorded cases. Health Secretary Robert F Kennedy Jr has been criticised by public health experts who argue his remarks and remaking of US vaccine policy has hindered efforts to stop the outbreaks. A vaccine skeptic, Kennedy has shared mixed messaging on the measles, mumps and rubella (MMR) vaccine, which has been proven safe and effective. The US recommends two shots to children between ages one and six, which is 97% effective in protecting against the illness. He has stated that the MMR vaccine is the "most effective way to prevent the spread of measles" but has also promoted unproven treatments. Earlier this year, he told lawmakers that a several countries have recently lost their measles elimination status, and defended the response by US officials. "The whole world had their worst measles year," he said. Earlier this year, a federal judge blocked the US government from implementing a series of sweeping changes to childhood immunisations under Kennedy's leadership, including slashing the number of recommended shots for children from 17 to 11. To achieve herd immunity, which limits the spread of measles and protects the unvaccinated, around 95% of the population must have the shots. But vaccination rates have dipped far below that in some areas. Last year, during an outbreak among the Russian and Ukrainian immigrant community in Spartanburg, South Carolina, some schools in the community had vaccination rates as low as 20%. 口服候选药物可阻断雪貂中的 传播 类 麻疹 疾病 - 新闻-医疗

Oral drug candidate blocks transmission of measles-like disease in ferrets - News-Medical
新闻简报佐治亚州立大学发表在《自然微生物学》上的一项开创性研究表明,口服 抗病毒药物 候选药物 GHP-88310 可以成功阻断雪貂中 传播 类 麻疹 疾病。在接触之前或之后服用该药物,可以防止直接接触和空气传播的 传播,同时减少临床症状。这项研究发布之际,麻疹 自 2025 年以来在美国重新出现,造成数千例病例和 3 人死亡,加拿大和墨西哥的 暴发 威胁到了北美的消除状态。该药物由 Richard Plemper 和 Carolin Lieber 等研究人员开发,显示出“非凡的 抗病毒药物 效力”,有可能增强传统的 疫苗接种 并减轻检疫负担。目前团队正在准备GHP-88310正式临床试验,为麻疹 暴发对照带来新的希望。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-07-24。证据中未指定事件日期。
地点与对象
研究在佐治亚州立大学 抗病毒药物 转化研究中心进行。研究将雪貂作为 麻疹 类疾病的模型。背景内容提到 麻疹 自 2025 年以来在美国重新出现,暴发 在加拿大和墨西哥重新出现。
事件
发表在《自然微生物学》上的一项研究表明,口服 抗病毒药物 候选药物 GHP-88310 可以阻断雪貂体内的犬瘟热病毒(一种类似 麻疹 的疾病)的 传播。该药物进行了预防性给药(暴露前或暴露后)和治疗性给药,表明它可以预防接触和空气传播的 传播 并减少临床症状。该研究由理查德·普伦珀和卡罗琳·利伯领导。
规模、影响与风险
研究表明,GHP-88310完全阻止了雪貂中空气传播的传播,并缩短了受感染动物的传染持续时间。该药物被描述为具有“非凡的 抗病毒药物 效力”。研究指出,自 2025 年以来,麻疹 在美国重新出现,造成数千例病例、数百人住院和三人死亡,加拿大和墨西哥也有大量 麻疹 挑战北美的 麻疹 消除状态。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究人员正在准备 GHP-88310 进行正式临床测试。该研究由美国国立卫生研究院 (NIH) 国家过敏和传染病研究所 (NIAID) 资助。该药物拟增强传统环 麻疹 对抗 麻疹 的作用,并有可能减轻长期隔离带来的社会和经济负担。证据来自同行评审期刊上发表的完整原始报告正文。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Oral drug candidate blocks transmission of measles-like disease in ferrets - News-Medical

News Brief
A groundbreaking study from Georgia State University, published in Nature Microbiology, reveals that an oral antiviral drug candidate, GHP-88310, successfully blocks transmission of a measles-like disease in ferrets. Administered either before or after exposure, the drug prevented both direct contact and airborne transmission while reducing clinical symptoms. This research comes amid a concerning reemergence of measles in the U.S. since 2025, with thousands of cases and three deaths, alongside outbreaks in Canada and Mexico threatening North America's elimination status. The drug, developed by researchers including Richard Plemper and Carolin Lieber, shows 'extraordinary antiviral potency' and could potentially augment traditional vaccination and reduce quarantine burdens. The team is now preparing GHP-88310 for formal clinical trials, offering new hope for measles outbreak control.
Original source text (English)
A new oral antiviral drug candidate administered either before or after direct contact or airborne exposure to canine distemper virus, which causes measles-like disease in ferrets, blocks transmission of the virus and reduces clinical symptoms, according to a study published in the journal Nature Microbiology by researchers in the Center for Translational Antiviral Research (CTAR) at Georgia State University. The study explored whether prophylactic (shortly before or after exposure) administration of the recently developed clinical candidate GHP-88310 (described in Science Advances), a broad-spectrum inhibitor of the viral polymerase, prevents virus transmission through close contact or through the air. The results demonstrate that GHP-88310 efficiently blocks both forms of viral spread. In addition, the study showed that treatment of infected animals shortened the time period in which infected animals could transmit the virus. Silencing measles outbreaks quickly is essential to reestablish control over the virus. This study follows our recent development of the drug candidate GHP-88310. It demonstrates that the drug is suitable to augment traditional ring vaccination against measles." Richard Plemper, senior author, a Regents' Professor and director of the CTAR Since 2025, measles has reemerged in the United States with thousands of cases across multiple states, hundreds of hospitalizations and three confirmed deaths. Large outbreaks in Canada and Mexico with multiple deaths have challenged the measles elimination status of North America. "We were very excited to see that GHP-88310 given by mouth completely prevented airborne transmission in our ferret model of measles," said first author Carolin Lieber, a senior postdoctoral fellow in the Plemper lab. "This finding is unprecedented for a viral polymerase inhibitor and demonstrates the extraordinary antiviral potency of this drug." To explore relevant conditions of viral transmission, the researchers developed a system that allowed them to pair infected and uninfected animals in direct physical contact or shared airspace, each under controlled environmental parameters. "We designed the study to recapitulate viral spread between people with direct contact, for instance in a household, and between more distant social contacts, for example in classrooms or other indoor settings that bring people into proximity without direct interaction," said Plemper. "In addition to this prophylactic benefit, GHP-88310 used therapeutically shortened the duration of disease in our model. If equally applicable to human hosts, it may shorten the severe social and economic burden of prolonged quarantine of patients and further aid outbreak management." The investigators are now readying GHP-88310 for formal clinical testing. Additional authors of the study include Josef Wolf, Claire Ruckel and Lauren Harrison of the Center for Translational Antiviral Research in the Institute for Biomedical Sciences at Georgia State. The study was funded by the National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases (NIAID) of the National Institutes of Health (NIH). Source: Journal reference: Lieber, C. M., et al . (2026). Antiviral GHP-88310 blocks contact-mediated and airborne transmission in a ferret model of measles-like disease. Nature Microbiology. DOI: 10.1038/s41564-026-02419-y. https://www.nature.com/articles/s41564-026-02419-y

A study published in Nature Microbiology demonstrates that an oral antiviral drug candidate, GHP-88310, blocks transmission of canine distemper virus (a measles-like disease) in ferrets. The drug was administered prophylactically (before or after exposure) and therapeutically, showing it prevents both contact and airborne transmission and reduces clinical symptoms. The research is led by Richard Plemper and Carolin Lieber.

The drug is described as having 'extraordinary antiviral potency.' The study notes that since 2025, measles has reemerged in the U.S. with thousands of cases, hundreds of hospitalizations, and three deaths, with large outbreaks also in Canada and Mexico challenging North America's measles elimination status.
Response, status and uncertainty
The researchers are readying GHP-88310 for formal clinical testing. The study was funded by the National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases (NIAID) of the NIH. The drug is proposed to augment traditional ring vaccination against measles and potentially reduce the social and economic burden of prolonged quarantine. The evidence is from a full original report body published in a peer-reviewed journal.

Oral antiviral GHP-88310 candidate shown to block measles transmission in ferret model - Drug Target Review
新闻简报2026 年 7 月 24 日,Drug Target Review 报道称,实验性口服 抗病毒药物(GHP-88310)已证明能够在雪貂体内预防 麻疹 替代病毒通过空气传播和接触传播的 传播。这一发现表明,GHP-88310 可以在 麻疹 暴发 期间作为 疫苗接种 的潜在补充。然而,该研究仅限于临床前模型,并未提供有关人类应用或现实世界影响的数据。证据中没有提及具体病例、死亡或暴露人数。该研究强调了 抗病毒药物 疗法的一个有希望的发展,但仍处于实验阶段,没有在人群中进行进一步验证。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Oral antiviral GHP-88310 candidate shown to block measles transmission in ferret model - Drug Target Review

News Brief
On July 24, 2026, Drug Target Review reported that an experimental oral antiviral, GHP-88310, has demonstrated the ability to prevent both airborne and contact-based transmission of a measles surrogate virus in ferrets. This finding suggests that GHP-88310 could serve as a potential complement to vaccination during measles outbreaks. However, the study is limited to a preclinical model and does not provide data on human application or real-world impact. No specific case, death, or exposure counts were mentioned in the evidence. The research highlights a promising development in antiviral therapy but remains in the experimental stage without further validation in human populations.
Original source text (English)

口服 抗病毒药物 可阻断雪貂中的 麻疹 样病毒 传播 - 基因工程和生物技术新闻

Oral Antiviral Blocks Measles-Like Virus Transmission in Ferrets - Genetic Engineering and Biotechnology News
新闻简报一种新的口服 抗病毒药物 候选药物 GHP-88310 在阻断雪貂中的 麻疹 样病毒 传播 方面显示出有希望的结果。佐治亚州立大学发表在《自然微生物学》上的研究表明,该药物可以预防接触介导的和空气传播的犬瘟热病毒(雪貂中的一种类似 麻疹 的病原体),并减少临床症状。在暴露之前或之后施用,GHP-88310 完全阻断雪貂模型中的 传播。由Carolin Lieber和Richard Plemper领导的这项研究还发现,治疗性治疗将传染期缩短了五天。该药物是一种广谱正副粘病毒聚合酶抑制剂,目前正在准备进行正式临床测试,有可能增强传统的 疫苗接种 并减轻人类 麻疹 暴发 的检疫负担。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-07-24。该研究结果发表在《自然微生物学》杂志上,但所提供的证据中并未报告该研究的具体事件日期。
地点与对象
该研究是在佐治亚州立大学 抗病毒药物 转化研究中心 (CTAR) 进行的。该研究使用雪貂作为 麻疹 类疾病的模型,受感染和未受感染的动物在受控环境参数下配对,直接身体接触或共享空域。
事件
一种新的口服 抗病毒药物 候选药物 GHP-88310 在由犬瘟热病毒引起的 麻疹 样疾病的雪貂模型中进行了测试。这项发表在《自然微生物学》上的研究表明,GHP-88310 可以阻断接触介导和空气传播的病毒 传播,并减少雪貂的临床症状。这项研究由佐治亚州立大学的卡罗琳·利伯博士和理查德·普莱姆珀博士领导。
规模、影响与风险
研究表明,暴露前和暴露后预防性施用 GHP-88310 可预防雪貂出现 传播。治疗性 治疗 将传染期缩短了五天,并提高了存活率。该药物被描述为具有“非凡的 抗病毒药物 效力”,正在准备进行正式临床测试。该研究没有报告雪貂模型的具体病例数或死亡数。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究表明,GHP-88310 适合增强传统环 麻疹 对抗 麻疹。研究人员现在正在准备对该药物进行正式的临床测试。该研究旨在概括不同环境(例如家庭和教室)中人与人之间的病毒传播情况。研究结果表明,如果适用于人类,该药物可以减轻长期隔离带来的社会和经济负担。来源是完整的原始报告机构,所提供的证据中没有提到未解决的限制。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Oral Antiviral Blocks Measles-Like Virus Transmission in Ferrets - Genetic Engineering and Biotechnology News

News Brief
A new oral antiviral drug candidate, GHP-88310, has shown promising results in blocking measles-like virus transmission in ferrets. Published in Nature Microbiology, the study from Georgia State University demonstrated that the drug prevented both contact-mediated and airborne transmission of canine distemper virus (a measles-like pathogen in ferrets) and reduced clinical symptoms. Administered before or after exposure, GHP-88310 completely blocked transmission in the ferret model. The research, led by Carolin Lieber and Richard Plemper, also found that therapeutic treatment shortened the contagious phase by five days. The drug, a broad-spectrum orthoparamyxovirus polymerase inhibitor, is now being prepared for formal clinical testing, with potential to augment traditional vaccination and reduce quarantine burdens in human measles outbreaks.
Original source text (English)
Home Topics Infectious Diseases Oral Antiviral Blocks Measles-Like Virus Transmission in Ferrets Measles virus particle, illustration. [KATERYNA KON / Science Photo Library / Getty Images] This year, the United States has recorded the highest number of measles cases since 2000, the year that the disease was declared eliminated from the country. As of mid-July, 2,260 measles cases were reported—just 29 cases less than the entire year of 2025. And outbreaks are widespread, with 34 new outbreaks reported in 2026. The reason for this surge in cases is waning vaccination rates across the country. Given that, the production of new antivirals is an urgent matter. Map of measles cases in the U.S. [CDC] Now, the new oral antiviral drug candidate GHP-88310 has shown promising results in a ferret model of infection. When administered before or after direct contact or airborne exposure to canine distemper virus (which causes measles-like disease in ferrets) GHP-88310 blocked transmission of the virus and reduced clinical symptoms in ferrets. “We were very excited to see that GHP-88310 given by mouth completely prevented airborne transmission in our ferret model of measles,” said Carolin Lieber, PhD, a postdoctoral fellow in the Plemper lab at Georgia State University. “This finding is unprecedented for a viral polymerase inhibitor and demonstrates the extraordinary antiviral potency of this drug.” This research is published in Nature Microbiology in the paper, “ Antiviral GHP-88310 blocks contact-mediated and airborne transmission in a ferret model of measles-like disease .” “Silencing measles outbreaks quickly is essential to reestablish control over the virus,” said Richard Plemper, PhD, professor and director of the Center for Translational Antiviral Research (CTAR) at Georgia State University. “This study follows our recent development of the drug candidate GHP-88310. It demonstrates that the drug is suitable to augment traditional ring vaccination against measles.” GHP-88310 (described earlier this year in Science Advances ) is known to be an orally efficacious broad-spectrum orthoparamyxovirus polymerase inhibitor. But its effect on viral transmission has remained unclear. This study explored whether prophylactic administration of GHP-88310 prevents virus transmission through close contact or through the air. The results demonstrate that GHP-88310 efficiently blocks both forms of viral spread. In addition, the study showed that treatment of infected animals shortened the time period in which infected animals could transmit the virus. To explore relevant conditions of viral transmission, the researchers established both direct-contact and airborne canine distemper virus transmission models to examine pharmacological suppression of virus spread. The transmission systems allowed them to pair infected and uninfected animals in direct physical contact or shared airspace, each under controlled environmental parameters. “We designed the study to recapitulate viral spread between people with direct contact, for instance in a household, and between more distant social contacts, for example in classrooms or other indoor settings that bring people into proximity without direct interaction,” said Plemper. The results showed that pre- and post-exposure prophylactic GHP-88310, given twice daily to air contacts, prevented transmission. The authors note that once-daily prophylactic administration mediated complete survival with all air contacts undergoing seroconversion. In addition, they note that therapeutic treatment of air contacts mitigated clinical signs, and animals survived, whereas all vehicle-treated air contacts succumbed. In addition, therapeutic treatment of infected source animals shortened the contagious phase by five days. “In addition to this prophylactic benefit, GHP-88310 used therapeutically shortened the duration of disease in our model,” noted Plemper. “If equally applicable to human hosts, it may shorten the severe social and economic burden of prolonged quarantine of patients and further aid outbreak management.” The investigators are now readying GHP-88310 for formal clinical testing. Time
Published on 2026-07-24. The study results were published in Nature Microbiology, but the specific event date of the research is not reported in the supplied evidence.
Location and population
The research was conducted at Georgia State University's Center for Translational Antiviral Research (CTAR). The study used ferrets as a model for measles-like disease, with infected and uninfected animals paired in direct physical contact or shared airspace under controlled environmental parameters.
Event
A new oral antiviral drug candidate, GHP-88310, was tested in a ferret model of measles-like disease caused by canine distemper virus. The study, published in Nature Microbiology, demonstrated that GHP-88310 blocked both contact-mediated and airborne transmission of the virus and reduced clinical symptoms in ferrets. The research was led by Carolin Lieber, PhD, and Richard Plemper, PhD, at Georgia State University.
Scale, impact and risk
The study showed that pre- and post-exposure prophylactic administration of GHP-88310 prevented transmission in ferrets. Therapeutic treatment shortened the contagious phase by five days and improved survival rates. The drug is described as having 'extraordinary antiviral potency' and is being prepared for formal clinical testing. The study does not report specific case counts or death counts for the ferret model.
Response, status and uncertainty
The research demonstrates that GHP-88310 is suitable to augment traditional ring vaccination against measles. The investigators are now readying the drug for formal clinical testing. The study was designed to recapitulate viral spread between people in various settings, such as households and classrooms. The findings suggest that if applicable to humans, the drug could shorten the social and economic burden of prolonged quarantine. The source is a full original report body, and there are no unresolved limitations mentioned in the supplied evidence.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

A new antiviral could stop measles before an outbreak takes off - ScienceDaily

News Brief
Researchers have developed an experimental pill that prevented a measles-like virus from spreading through the air or close contact in ferrets, according to a study reported by ScienceDaily on July 24, 2026. The antiviral, when given shortly before or after exposure, also shortened the duration of illness and the contagious period in infected animals. While the findings are promising for potential future use in measles outbreak prevention, the research is still at a preclinical stage. No human trials have been conducted, and the compound has not been approved for use. The study highlights a possible new approach to controlling measles transmission, but further research is needed to determine efficacy and safety in humans.

在一些 麻疹 病例中的有限合作使得俄勒冈州更难控制该病毒

Limited cooperation in some measles cases is making the virus harder to contain in Oregon
新闻简报俄勒冈州正经历一次麻疹暴发,受影响家庭有限度的合作使防控工作复杂化。在最近的一起事件中,两名感染儿童不顾公共卫生指示前往波特兰地区学校,导致40名未接种疫苗的学生和教职员工暴露,随后被隔离三周。这反映了社区与卫生官员之间更广泛的信任挑战,特别是俄勒冈州在2026年迄今已报告30例麻疹病例和2例住院病例。公共卫生部门因病例增加和资金削减而承受压力,官员们敦促在学年开始前进行疫苗接种。麻疹病毒仍具有高度传染性,病毒可在空气中停留长达两小时,并可能使10个未受保护的人中的9人感染。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-07-23。事件发生在 2026 年春季。
地点与对象
俄勒冈州摩特诺玛县波特兰地区学校。受影响人群:40 名未接种疫苗的学生和教职员工。
事件
确认了 麻疹 麻疹,受影响家庭的配合有限。两名受感染的儿童不顾公共卫生部门的指示留在家中上学,导致 40 名未接种疫苗的人受到感染。
规模、影响与风险
40 名未接种疫苗的学生和工作人员暴露并被隔离三周。俄勒冈州在 2026 年报告了 30 例 麻疹 病例和 2 例住院治疗。麻疹 具有高度传染性,每 10 名未受保护的人中多达 9 人被传染者感染。
应对、状态与不确定性
由于不信任和资源有限,公共卫生官员面临挑战。摩特诺玛县卫生官员理查德·布鲁诺博士报告了接触者追踪和隔离方面的困难。由于病例增加和资金削减,公共卫生部门捉襟见肘。废水数据表明病例数可能被低估。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Limited cooperation in some measles cases is making the virus harder to contain in Oregon

News Brief
Oregon is experiencing a measles outbreak with limited cooperation from affected households complicating containment efforts. In a recent incident, two infected children attended a Portland-area school despite public health instructions to stay home, exposing 40 unvaccinated students and staff who were subsequently quarantined for three weeks. This reflects a broader challenge of mistrust between communities and health officials, particularly as Oregon has reported 30 measles cases and 2 hospitalizations so far in 2026. Public health departments are strained by increased cases and funding cuts, with officials urging vaccination before the school year. Measles remains highly contagious, with the virus remaining airborne for up to two hours and potentially infecting 9 out of 10 unprotected individuals.
Original source text (English)
This spring, two children attended a Portland-area school while infected with measles. Both were exposed at home and were showing symptoms. The kids were contacts of a known measles case that public health officials were monitoring. Adults in the household were told to keep the children home from school if they developed symptoms. But, according to Multnomah County Health Officer Dr. Richard Bruno, that didn’t happen, and the children were sent to school without public health’s knowledge. As a result, 40 unvaccinated students and staff at the school were exposed to the virus and forced into quarantine for three weeks. The story is an example, Bruno said, of how mistrust between people falling ill with measles and public health workers is making it challenging to stop transmission of the virus in Oregon — and nationwide. Measles is easily preventable with a vaccine, but there is no antiviral treatment for it. An infection can lead to hospitalization and lifelong complications, particularly for children under 5. In the past 18 months, the United States has had more reported measles cases than in the previous 25 years combined. There have been 2,260 confirmed measles cases so far this year, with the overwhelming majority from local transmission of the virus. Only a handful of cases are from international travel. Bruno first shared details about the school exposure in remarks on Tuesday at a news conference of the Big Cities Health Coalition, a not-for-profit group that represents 36 of the largest health departments across the country and lobbies for public health funding. The group is urging families to get their children up to date on their measles vaccines before the upcoming school year. Public health departments, they said, are stretched thin by a historic increase in measles cases, emerging threats like Andes virus and the cyclospora outbreak, and federal funding cuts. In Multnomah County, the lost funding includes cuts to a federal grant that had supported outreach and vaccination campaigns to reduce racial and ethnic health disparities. “I think we’re all hitting challenging budget years in our health departments,” Bruno said. “We have less staff to organize vaccination events and interface with community on the importance of protecting themselves and their loved ones.” Bruno didn’t name the school in the anecdote he shared. In April, the Oregon Health Authority reported repeated measles exposures at Centennial Middle School in Southeast Portland, prompting a major public health response. In other instances, the county has had better luck building rapport with unvaccinated people. In April, during a measles outbreak that sickened farmworkers, nurses from Multnomah County and neighboring counties vaccinated more than 150 farmworkers during a vaccine event at a farm. Oregon has reported 30 cases of measles and two hospitalizations due to the virus so far this year. Oregon’s state health officer has previously said that number is likely an undercount — wastewater data suggests people with mild infections may be spreading measles undetected. Nearly all of Oregon’s confirmed cases were in people who were unvaccinated or had unknown vaccination status. Two were people who had been vaccinated. Another challenge amid the resurgence of the virus, officials said, has been limiting exposures in hospitals and clinics. Earlier this month, the Oregon Health Authority reported that people were exposed to measles at two Providence hospitals and an urgent care facility. Public health officials ask households with known measles cases to call ahead of time if a new family member develops symptoms and needs to come in for care or testing, Bruno explained, but the message doesn’t always get through. The best practice, he said, is for the family to call their regular provider and explain that they need a measles test. Then, the patient can remain in their car while they are swabbed, getting the test they need without exposing anyone else. Limiting exposures is critical because measles is generally considered the most contagious respiratory virus. While vaccinated people or people with immunity are unlikely to get it, a contagious person who coughs or sneezes will infect up to 9 out of 10 unprotected people around them, and the virus can remain in the air for up to two hours. Another challenge is the course that measles typically takes. The first symptoms are a high fever, cough, runny nose and red eyes — symptoms common to several respiratory viruses that might not signal a measles diagnosis. People can later develop a rash that starts from their head and spreads downwards. By that point, they may have been contagious for up to four days. Bruno said if people know they were around someone who was potentially exposed to measles, or have spent time with someone who was unusually sick, that’s important information to share with healthcare providers. Other clues that a childhood illness could be measles include recent travel through an airport — even for domestic travel — and visiting large venues like theme parks that are popular with international travelers

Time
Published on 2026-07-23. Event occurred in spring 2026.
Location and population
Portland-area school in Multnomah County, Oregon. Affected population: 40 unvaccinated students and staff.
Event
Confirmed measles outbreak with limited cooperation from affected households. Two infected children attended school despite public health instructions to stay home, resulting in exposure of 40 unvaccinated individuals.
Scale, impact and risk
40 unvaccinated students and staff exposed and quarantined for three weeks. Oregon reported 30 measles cases and 2 hospitalizations in 2026. Measles is highly contagious, with up to 9 out of 10 unprotected people infected by a contagious person.
Response, status and uncertainty
Public health officials are facing challenges due to mistrust and limited resources. Multnomah County Health Officer Dr. Richard Bruno reported difficulties in contact tracing and isolation. Public health departments are stretched thin due to increased cases and funding cuts. Wastewater data suggests potential undercounting of cases.

新的 抗病毒药物 可以在 麻疹 起飞之前阻止 麻疹

A new antiviral could stop measles before an outbreak takes off
新闻简报佐治亚州立大学的研究人员开发了一种实验性 抗病毒药物 药丸,可以通过直接接触和空气传播途径防止雪貂之间传播类似 麻疹 的病毒。治疗(GHP-88310)在暴露之前或之后给药也可以减轻症状并缩短传染期。这项研究发表在《自然微生物学》上,正值 麻疹 自 2025 年以来在北美重新出现,导致数千例感染、数百例住院治疗和 3 例死亡。研究人员指出,快速沉默 麻疹 对于重新控制病毒至关重要,并表明该药物可以补充传统的 疫苗接种 策略。该团队目前正在为正式临床试验准备候选药物。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026 年 7 月 24 日。事件日期:未明确说明,但研究结果发表在《自然微生物学》上。
地点与对象
研究在佐治亚州立大学 抗病毒药物 转化研究中心进行。研究人群:感染犬瘟热病毒(一种 麻疹 样病毒)的雪貂。对北美人群的影响,自 2025 年以来就有 麻疹 暴发 的报道。
事件
实验性 抗病毒药物 药丸 GHP-88310 可通过直接接触和空气传播途径预防雪貂之间的 传播 类 麻疹 病毒。在接触之前或之后服用 治疗 还可以减轻症状并缩短传染期。佐治亚州立大学科学家在《自然微生物学》上发表的研究成果。
规模、影响与风险
在雪貂模型中进行的研究;没有报告人类病例数。该药物可阻断接触介导的 传播 和空气传播的 传播。自 2025 年以来,麻疹 在北美已造成数千例感染、数百例住院治疗和三例确诊死亡,引发了人们对维持消除状态的担忧。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究人员正在准备GHP-88310进行正式临床试验。该药物被描述为传统环 疫苗接种 的潜在补充。该研究成功模拟了现实的 传播 条件,模仿了家庭和教室环境。来源中没有报告当前的限制。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

A new antiviral could stop measles before an outbreak takes off

News Brief
Researchers at Georgia State University have developed an experimental antiviral pill that prevented transmission of a measles-like virus between ferrets through both direct contact and airborne routes. The treatment, GHP-88310, also reduced symptoms and shortened the contagious period when administered before or after exposure. Published in Nature Microbiology, the study comes as measles has reemerged in North America, causing thousands of infections, hundreds of hospitalizations, and three deaths since 2025. The researchers note that silencing outbreaks quickly is essential to regain control over the virus and suggest the drug could complement traditional vaccination strategies. The team is now preparing the drug candidate for formal clinical trials.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Your source for the latest research news Follow: Facebook X/Twitter Subscribe: RSS Feeds Newsletter New! Sign up for our free email newsletter. Science News from research organizations A new antiviral could stop measles before an outbreak takes off Date: July 24, 2026 Source: Georgia State University Summary: An experimental pill prevented a measles-like virus from spreading through the air or through close contact in ferrets. It worked when given shortly before or after exposure and also shortened illness and the period when infected animals remained contagious. Researchers say the drug could one day complement vaccination and make outbreaks easier to contain. Share: Facebook Twitter Pinterest LinkedIN Email FULL STORY An experimental antiviral pill stopped a measles-like virus from spreading through the air, offering a potentially powerful new weapon against outbreaks. Credit: Shutterstock Researchers at Georgia State University have developed an experimental oral antiviral that prevented a measles-like virus from spreading between ferrets through both close contact and the air. The treatment also reduced symptoms when it was given either shortly before or shortly after exposure. The findings, published in Nature Microbiology, come from scientists at the Center for Translational Antiviral Research (CTAR). Their work focused on canine distemper virus, which produces a disease in ferrets that closely resembles measles. Antiviral Blocks Two Routes of Transmission The researchers tested a recently developed drug candidate called GHP-88310 (described in Science Advances). The medication is a broad-spectrum inhibitor of the viral polymerase, an enzyme the virus needs in order to reproduce. The team examined whether prophylactic (shortly before or after exposure) use of the drug could prevent transmission through direct contact or shared air. GHP-88310 successfully blocked both forms of spread. When the drug was used to treat animals that were already infected, it also reduced the length of time they remained capable of transmitting the virus. "Silencing measles outbreaks quickly is essential to reestablish control over the virus," said senior author Richard Plemper, a Regents' Professor and director of the CTAR. "This study follows our recent development of the drug candidate GHP-88310. It demonstrates that the drug is suitable to augment traditional ring vaccination against measles." Measles Outbreaks Return Across North America Measles has reemerged in the United States since 2025, causing thousands of infections across several states, hundreds of hospitalizations and three confirmed deaths. Canada and Mexico have also experienced major outbreaks involving multiple deaths, raising concerns about whether North America can maintain its measles elimination status. "We were very excited to see that GHP-88310 given by mouth completely prevented airborne transmission in our ferret model of measles," said first author Carolin Lieber, a senior postdoctoral fellow in the Plemper lab. "This finding is unprecedented for a viral polymerase inhibitor and demonstrates the extraordinary antiviral potency of this drug." Tests Mimicked Homes and Classrooms To model realistic transmission conditions, the researchers created a controlled system in which infected and uninfected animals either came into direct physical contact or shared the same airspace without touching. "We designed the study to recapitulate viral spread between people with direct contact, for instance in a household, and between more distant social contacts, for example in classrooms or other indoor settings that bring people into proximity without direct interaction," said Plemper. "In addition to this prophylactic benefit, GHP-88310 used therapeutically shortened the duration of disease in our model. If equally applicable to human hosts, it may shorten the severe social and economic burden of prolonged quarantine of patients and further aid outbreak management." Drug Candidate Moves Toward Clinical Testing The researchers are now preparing GHP-88310 for formal clinical trials. Other contributors to the study included Josef Wolf, Claire Ruckel and Lauren Harrison of the Center for Translational Antiviral Research in the Institute for Biomedical Sciences at Georgia State. The research was supported by the National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases (NIAID) of the National Institutes of Health (NIH). Measles Cases Hit New Record in U.S., as Vaccination Rates Wane - The New York Times

News Brief
The United States has reported a record number of measles cases in the last two years, surpassing the total cases recorded from 2000 through 2024 combined. This surge coincides with declining vaccination rates, highlighting a significant public health concern. The outbreak underscores the importance of maintaining high immunization coverage to prevent the spread of measles, a highly contagious viral disease. Health officials may need to intensify vaccination campaigns and public education efforts to reverse this trend and protect vulnerable populations.

Why are measles cases rising in the US? 2026 outbreak hits highest level in decades - Bergen Record
新闻简报美国将在 2026 年经历一次重大的 麻疹 麻疹,截至 7 月下旬已报告确诊病例超过 2,200 例,这是自 1990 年代初以来的最高年度数字。已有 40 多个州发现了病例,暴发 发病率较低的社区中出现了较大的 暴发 病例。卫生官员将这一上升归因于 MMR 疫苗接种 覆盖率下降,该覆盖率已低于群体免疫所需的 95% 阈值。这种具有高度传染性的病毒通过空气传播,并且可以持续存在,如果 传播 继续下去,美国将面临失去 2000 年实现的 麻疹 消除状态的风险。专家强调,疫苗接种 仍然是最有效的预防感染的保护措施。
