B routine priorityPublic-health news

Why are measles cases rising in the US? 2026 outbreak hits highest level in decades - Bergen Record

News Brief
The United States is experiencing a significant measles outbreak in 2026, with over 2,200 confirmed cases reported as of late July, marking the highest annual count since the early 1990s. Cases have been identified in more than 40 states, with larger outbreaks occurring in communities with lower vaccination rates. Health officials attribute the rise to declining MMR vaccination coverage, which has fallen below the 95% threshold needed for herd immunity. The highly contagious virus spreads through the air and can linger, posing a risk of the U.S. losing its measles elimination status achieved in 2000 if transmission continues. Experts emphasize that vaccination remains the most effective protection against infection.
Original source text (English)
News Bergen Sports HS Sports Real Estate Advertise Obituaries eNewspaper Legals NEWSWhy are measles cases rising in the US? 2026 outbreak hits highest level in decades Maria FrancisUSA TODAY NETWORKJuly 24, 2026, 10:23 a.m. ETA disease that the United States declared eliminated more than two decades ago is making an alarming comeback. What to know about the measles outbreaks right now.More than 2,200 measles cases have already been confirmed nationwide in 2026, surpassing the total reported during all of 2025 and marking the highest annual case count since the early 1990s, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. Health officials say declining vaccination rates in some communities have allowed one of the world's most contagious viruses to regain a foothold.The outbreaks have stretched across dozens of states, raising concerns that the U.S. could eventually lose its measles elimination status if transmission continues for an extended period.How many measles cases are there in 2026?As of late July, more than 2,200 confirmed cases had been reported in the United States, exceeding the total number recorded during all of 2025. Cases have been identified in more than 40 states, including New Jersey, Pennsylvania and Washington, D.C.Many of the largest outbreaks have occurred in communities with lower vaccination rates. Health experts note that measles can spread rapidly because the virus is highly contagious and can linger in the air after an infected person leaves an area.Why are measles cases rising?Public health officials point largely to falling vaccination coverage.Nationwide measles, mumps and rubella (MMR) vaccination rates among kindergarten-age children have slipped below the roughly 95% threshold experts say is needed to maintain strong community protection."High vaccination coverage has protected even the un- and undervaccinated because of herd protection, keeping measles at bay for the past few decades," Dr. William Moss of Johns Hopkins said. "But in communities with pockets of susceptible individuals across a broad age range, measles has been able to take hold."According to CDC-linked and Johns Hopkins tracking data, the overwhelming majority of cases have occurred among people who were unvaccinated or whose vaccination status was unknown.Could the US lose its measles elimination status?Possibly.The United States achieved measles elimination status in 2000, meaning the virus was no longer spreading continuously within the country. But experts warn that prolonged outbreaks could threaten that designation."This indicates that we have not been able to shut down measles virus transmission in this country," Moss told Science News, pointing to under-vaccinated communities scattered across the U.S.For now, health officials continue urging people to verify they are up to date on recommended measles vaccinations, describing it as the most effective protection against infection.What is measles and how do people get it?Measles is a highly contagious virus that spreads through the air when an infected person coughs, sneezes or even breathes. Symptoms usually begin with a high fever, cough, runny nose and red, watery eyes before a distinctive red rash appears and spreads across the body.You can catch measles by being in the same room as an infected person, even up to two hours after they've left. Because the virus spreads so easily, health experts say it can quickly move through communities where vaccination rates are low. The measles, mumps and rubella (MMR) vaccine is considered the best protection against infection.Maria Francis is a Pennsylvania-based journalist covering trending and breaking topics across the Mid-Atlantic and Northeast regions for USA Today Network. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Measles surge hits new milestone as US cases surpass last year's total

News Brief
The U.S. has reached a new milestone in its measles outbreak, with 2026 cases totaling 2,295, surpassing last year's 2,286 cases. Concentrated outbreaks have been reported in multiple states, with Texas experiencing the largest outbreak in 2025. Most cases involve unvaccinated individuals, primarily affecting children and young adults. While no deaths have been reported this year, health officials warn of potential loss of the U.S. measles elimination status due to sustained local transmission. The CDC emphasizes the importance of MMR vaccination to maintain herd immunity, with experts stressing that 95% coverage is needed to protect vulnerable populations.
Original source text (English)
Measles has reemerged as a persistent public health challenge in the U.S. over the past two years — and the spread isn't slowing down. According to a Johns Hopkins University tracker that compiles local and state health data , there were 2,295 confirmed measles cases in the U.S. as of July 22. This figure has officially surpassed the 2025 numbers, which totaled 2,286 measles cases nationwide. Measles-infected Traveler May Have Exposed Passengers At Lax And Nearby Hotel, Health Officials Warn Cases have been reported across the country, but concentrated outbreaks have worsened in some areas of Pennsylvania, South Carolina , Florida, Virginia, Utah, Texas and Arizona. In 2025, Texas had the largest outbreak, totaling 803 cases throughout the state. Measles cases have officially surpassed 2025 numbers, which totaled 2,286 cases. The U.S. has reported 2,295 measles cases so far in 2026. In the latest CDC measles update on July 17, there were 34 new outbreaks reported in 2026, compared to 48 outbreaks in 2025. Most cases were associated with local outbreaks, while some were linked to international visitors. Test Yourself With Our Latest Lifestyle Quiz Both this year and last year, 93% of all measles patients were either unvaccinated for measles, mumps and rubella (MMR), or had an unknown vaccination status , data shows. Most cases have occurred in children and young adults between the ages of 5 and 19. There have been no deaths reported in 2026, according to the CDC, but there were three deaths in 2025. The rise in cases comes more than two decades after health agencies reported that the disease was eliminated in 2000. Measles can be spread through direct contact with infectious droplets , or can be airborne when an infected person breathes, coughs or sneezes, according to health officials. The virus can remain infectious in the air for up to two hours after an infected person leaves an area. Click Here To Sign Up For Our Health Newsletter Symptoms usually appear within a week or two, and may include a high fever, cough, runny nose and red, watery eyes. A few days later, flat, red spots appear on the face and then spread down the neck to the rest of the body. People are most contagious in the four days before and four days after the rash appears. Dr. Marc Siegel , Fox News senior medical analyst, emphasized the highly contagious nature of measles. "Measles is so contagious that up to 90% of susceptible people who are exposed to an infected person will become infected," he told Fox News Digital, noting the importance of community protection. "We need a 95% vaccine coverage rate to ensure herd immunity," he said. The herd immunity threshold is a mathematical estimate based on how contagious a disease is and how effectively immunity from vaccination or prior infection prevents its spread, according to Siegel. "In this case, two-dose vaccination is almost 100% effective, and natural immunity after a measles infection is generally lifelong," the doctor said. "This isn't about personal choice, it's about community protection. We need to protect those who can't have a live virus vaccine because they are immunocompromised or pregnant." The doctor also cautioned that the U.S. could be at risk of losing its measles elimination status, as the number and duration of outbreaks suggest sustained local transmission within the U.S., rather than isolated cases linked only to international travel. Click Here To Download The Fox News App The CDC recommends that everyone receive two doses of the MMR vaccine, calling vaccination the best protection against measles. Anyone who is showing measles symptoms or thinks they've been exposed should self-isolate and contact their doctor before showing up to a medical center and infecting others. B routine priorityPublic-health news

MAP: States report new measles cases as virus surpasses alarming benchmark

News Brief
The U.S. is experiencing a significant measles outbreak with 2,318 confirmed cases across 43 states, surpassing the 2025 record of 2,289 cases. The CDC reports the outbreak has spread to all but seven states, with experts warning the actual number of cases could be much higher, particularly in the Utah-Arizona border region. This surge has put the country at risk of losing its measles elimination status held since 2000. Public health officials emphasize the virus's extreme contagiousness, noting that 9 out of 10 unvaccinated people exposed will become infected. Authorities are urging increased vaccination rates to curb the spread, as the outbreak continues to impact communities nationwide.
Original source text (English)
(NEXSTAR) – Cyclospora isn't the only public health crisis the U.S. is contending with in 2026. Measles, one of the world's most contagious viruses, has now spread to 43 states, the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention said on Friday. With months left in the year, the country has already surpassed the record-breaking number of measles cases we saw in all of 2025. The CDC has confirmed 2,318 cases of measles across all but seven U.S. states. The nation had 2,289 cases in 2025, the worst year for measles in more than three decades . Now, 2026 has easily become the U.S.'s worst year for measles since 1991. Over 11,000 cases of cyclosporiasis suspected in US: The states seeing spikes Early in the year, the biggest outbreak was easily in South Carolina . In the past few months, hundreds of measles cases have been confirmed in Arizona, Florida, Pennsylvania, Texas, Utah and Virginia. The only states without confirmed cases – at least as of Thursday – are Arkansas, Hawaii, Indiana, Mississippi, Nevada, New Hampshire and West Virginia. But public health departments and disease researchers have long acknowledged that official counts are incomplete, because some people get sick and don't seek out treatment. Experts relying on mathematical modeling and genetic analyses indicate that a large outbreak on the Utah-Arizona border could be around three to four times bigger than what's currently known, state epidemiologist Dr. Leisha Nolen said. Nearly 1.6 million cartons of eggs recalled over salmonella concerns: Full list of products impacted The U.S. is now at risk of losing its measles elimination status , a designation it has held since 2000. "Regardless of what is determined about the country's measles elimination status, the reality is that the United States has more measles cases this year than it has in decades," said Dr. William Moss, an epidemiology professor at the Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health and director of the school's International Vaccine Access Center. "We must focus on improving access to measles vaccine and increasing immunization coverage to stop measles in its tracks." There is little room for error in trying to stop measles. The virus is one of the most contagious, infecting 9 out of every 10 unvaccinated people exposed. A person with measles who coughs or sneezes in a room, and then leaves that room, can still infect another person who enters that room up to two hours later, the CDC says. Community-level protection takes a 95% vaccination rate. Some people liken skipping vaccination to deciding not to wear a seat belt in a car, but that's not a perfect analogy, said Dr. Mark Roberts, retired director of the Public Health Dynamics Lab at the University of Pittsburgh School of Public Health. Some people, like small infants and those with weakened immune systems, can't get immunity through a vaccine. "Not vaccinating your kid is like saying, 'I don't want my car to have brakes,'" he said. "Because it's other people that you can hit. It's not just you in your car. It's other people." The Associated Press contributed to this report. Copyright 2026 Nexstar Media, Inc. Measles cases in the U.S. hit a new 34-year high - Science News

News Brief
Science News reported on July 22, 2026, that measles cases in the United States have reached a new 34-year high. The article highlights the resurgence of the disease, emphasizing the need for public health attention. However, it does not provide specific numbers of cases, deaths, or exposures, nor does it detail the health system's response or risk levels. The report is based on general observations and does not include confirmed or suspected data about the outbreak. The article serves as a call to action for addressing the spread of measles, particularly in light of recent trends and the potential impact on public health.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Delaware declares measles outbreak as cases rise to 4 statewide - Delawareonline.com

News Brief
Delaware has declared a measles outbreak after four unvaccinated adult men in Kent County tested positive for the virus. The first case was confirmed on July 20, with three additional cases reported by July 22, meeting the CDC's outbreak definition of three or more cases connected by time and location. Public health officials are conducting extensive contact tracing to identify potential exposures. Measles, a highly contagious viral illness, poses severe risks to unvaccinated individuals, pregnant women, the immunocompromised, and infants too young for vaccination. While there is no specific treatment, post-exposure vaccination or antibody administration may be recommended for certain high-risk groups. The health department has not released further details due to privacy restrictions.
Original source text (English)
HEALTH Delaware measles cases rise to 4; health officials declare outbreak Isabel Hughes Delaware News Journal July 22, 2026 Updated July 23, 2026, 1:29 p.m. ET The Delaware Division of Public Health has declared a measles outbreak after four people total have now tested positive for the virus. The first positive case was confirmed on July 20. A second came the following day, and two more were confirmed in a July 22 news release. Per the division, a measles outbreak is defined as three or more cases connected by time and location. The definition comes from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. While details about the cases are limited, DPH said "extensive contact tracing is ongoing" to identify anyone exposed. Who has tested positive for measles in Delaware? The Division of Public Health said all four people who have tested positive are unvaccinated adult men in Kent County. The health department cannot give more information "due to medical privacy restrictions." Who is most at risk during a measles outbreak? People who are unvaccinated, pregnant, immunocompromised or too young to be vaccinated face the highest risk of severe complications, DPH said. The first dose for infants is usually given between 12 and 15 months. Anyone who cannot get the vaccine but who has come into contact with someone suspected to have measles should immediately contact their primary care provider or Delaware's Office of Infectious Disease Epidemiology at (302) 744-4990 for guidance. Are adults who were vaccinated decades ago still protected? If you were born before 1957, you are considered immune. If you were fully vaccinated, you’re also considered immune. If you’re concerned, however, your doctor can order a blood test to check for immunity. Such testing is fairly common. What are measles? Measles is a highly contagious, acute viral illness that begins with early symptoms of fever, cough, runny nose and conjunctivitis (pink eye), lasting two to four days before rash onset. The rash typically occurs three to five days after symptoms begin and usually appears on the face and spreads down the body. Measles can cause severe health complications, including pneumonia, brain inflammation and death. The virus is transmitted by direct contact with infectious droplets or by airborne spread when an infected person breathes, coughs or sneezes. The measles virus can remain infectious in the air and on surfaces for up to two hours after an infected person leaves an area. Infected people are contagious from four days before the rash starts through four days after rash development. The incubation period for measles from exposure to fever is usually about 10 days, with a range of seven to 12 days. And from exposure to rash onset is usually about 14 days with a seven to 21-day range. How do you treat measles? There is no specific antiviral cure for the measles . However, if you haven't been previously vaccinated against the measles , you can still be vaccinated to head off the worst symptoms, if you act fast enough. "If people older than six months of age are exposed to measles and are not immune by previous illness or vaccination, they can get a measles vaccine for up to three days after contact with the virus," advised the Mayo Clinic . "After the vaccine you don't need to stay home, also called quarantine." Antibodies can also be given for up to six days after exposure, though they are prioritized for people who cannot get the measles vaccine, such as infants younger than 6 months old, pregnant women, severely immunocompromised people or people who have other medical contraindications to vaccination, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. Got a story tip or idea? Send to Isabel Hughes at ihughes@delawareonline.com. Featured Weekly Ad

Event
Confirmed measles outbreak declared by Delaware Division of Public Health after four cases met the CDC definition of three or more cases connected by time and location.
Scale, impact and risk
Deaths: Not reported. Exposures: Under investigation via contact tracing. Risk: High for unvaccinated, pregnant, immunocompromised, or too young to be vaccinated individuals.
Response, status and uncertainty

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

麻疹 暴发 在特拉华州声明;宾夕法尼亚州切斯特县的病例也在增加 - 6abc Philadelphia

Measles outbreak declared in Delaware; cases also rising in Chester County, Pa. - 6abc Philadelphia
新闻简报特拉华州和宾夕法尼亚州的卫生官员正在对 麻疹 暴发 做出回应,特拉华州在肯特县出现四例未接种疫苗的成年男性确诊病例后宣布了 暴发。切斯特县报告了 13 例确诊病例,预计还会有更多病例。官员们正在开展接触者追踪并扩大 疫苗接种 的访问范围,强调 疫苗 的有效性为 97%。敦促居民接种疫苗,特别是婴儿和免疫功能低下者等弱势群体。暴发 强调了对 疫苗接种 发病率下降以及可能出现严重并发症(包括死亡)的担忧。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年7月23日。活动日期:周三(2026年7月22日)声明暴发。
地点与对象
特拉华州(肯特县)和宾夕法尼亚州切斯特县。受影响人群:特拉华州未接种疫苗的成年男性;两个地区的居民。
事件
特拉华州宣布确诊 麻疹 麻疹,其中有 4 例未接种疫苗的成年男性病例。宾夕法尼亚州切斯特县的病例也在增加(已确诊 13 例)。
规模、影响与风险
特拉华州:4例确诊病例。切斯特县:确诊病例 13 例,预计还会有更多病例。卫生官员警告称,可能会出现严重并发症和死亡。未明确说明风险级别。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生官员对其他病例进行广泛的接触者追踪和监测。特拉华州通过诊所、移动单位、药房和初级保健办公室扩大 疫苗接种 的使用范围。官方敦促疫苗接种,强调疫苗接种全系列的有效性超过97%。正在进行调查以确定 传播 的来源。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Measles outbreak declared in Delaware; cases also rising in Chester County, Pa. - 6abc Philadelphia

News Brief
Health officials in Delaware and Pennsylvania are responding to a measles outbreak, with Delaware declaring an outbreak after four confirmed cases in unvaccinated adult men in Kent County. Chester County reports 13 confirmed cases and expects more. Officials are conducting contact tracing and expanding vaccination access, emphasizing the vaccine's 97% effectiveness. Residents are urged to vaccinate, particularly vulnerable groups like infants and immunocompromised individuals. The outbreak highlights concerns about declining vaccination rates and the potential for severe complications, including death.
Original source text (English)
24/7 LivePhiladelphiaPennsylvaniaNew JerseyDelaware83° MeaslesMeasles outbreak declared in Delaware; cases also rising in Chester County, Pa.ByBriana Smith Thursday, July 23, 2026 12:13PMMeasles outbreak declared in Delaware; cases also rising in ChescoDOVER, Del. (WPVI) -- Health officials in Delaware and Chester County, Pennsylvania, are urging residents to get vaccinated as measles cases continue to climb across the region.Delaware authorities formally declared a measles outbreak on Wednesday after confirming four cases, all involving unvaccinated adult men in Kent County, Delaware.Dr. Robert Rosenbaum, medical director of the Delaware EMS and Preparedness section, said officials are still working to determine how the patients contracted the airborne virus."Measles is a highly contagious disease. It's why we vaccinate for it," he said.RELATED: Investigation: Big drop in MMR vaccinations at area schoolsRosenbaum added that investigators are conducting "extensive contact tracing for the patients we identified, and we're watching to see if there are any additional cases."Chester County's public health director reported 13 confirmed measles cases and anticipates more.Residents expressed concern about the growing numbers."It's concerning, and I wouldn't be surprised if the numbers go up," said Taylor Williams of Claymont, Del.ALSO SEE: New map shows how to spot the measles risk level in your ZIP codeHealth leaders warn that measles can lead to severe complications and even death. They continue to encourage vaccination to protect vulnerable groups, including infants, older adults and people who are immunocompromised.Rosenbaum emphasized the effectiveness of the vaccine, noting that "the immunity and protection rate with the full vaccination series is well over 97%. If you're vaccinated, you are extremely well protected."He said individuals are considered immune if they were born before 1957, have lab-confirmed immunity, or received two doses of the vaccine between ages 12 and 15 months and again between ages 4 and 6.Some residents voiced support for vaccination efforts."I feel it's the right thing to do, but everybody has their own choice in what they have to do, and they have to live with their decisions," said Joseph Morris of Ocean City, NJ.Rosenbaum said Delaware is working to expand vaccination access through clinics and mobile units.Vaccines are also available at pharmacies and primary care offices. Early symptoms of measles include fever, cough, runny nose and pink eye. Health officials advise anyone experiencing these signs to seek immediate care.Report a correction or typoCopyright © 2026 WPVI-TV. U.S. measles cases reach 35-year high in 2026, surpassing last year’s total - NBC News

News Brief
U.S. measles cases have reached a 35-year high in 2026, surpassing the total from the previous year, according to NBC News. The outbreak occurs amid a decades-long decline in childhood vaccination rates, though specific case numbers and response measures are not detailed in the report. The grim milestone highlights ongoing public health challenges related to preventable infectious diseases in the United States.

德克萨斯州 麻疹:卫生官员称蒙哥马利县确诊 4 例 麻疹 - ABC13 Houston

Measles in Texas: 4 cases of measles confirmed in Montgomery County, health officials say - ABC13 Houston
新闻简报得克萨斯州蒙哥马利县卫生官员已确认四例 麻疹 病例,涉及四名家庭成员,他们曾接触过一名来自州外的旅行者。一名 20 至 30 岁的妇女正在住院。没有一个感染者接种过疫苗。麻疹 是一种高度传染性的病毒感染,可导致包括脑炎在内的严重并发症,特别是对于幼儿。该病毒通过空气传播,引起发烧、咳嗽、流鼻涕和皮疹等症状。卫生当局敦促 疫苗接种 防止进一步传播,并正在进行接触者追踪以遏制 暴发。

德克萨斯州蒙哥马利县。受影响人群:四名家庭成员,其中一名20至30岁的女性正在住院治疗。
事件
已确认德克萨斯州蒙哥马利县的 麻疹 的 暴发。蒙哥马利县公共卫生部门确认了四起病例,涉及四名家庭成员,他们曾接触过来自州外的旅行者。没有一个感染者接种过疫苗。
规模、影响与风险
住院治疗:1 例(20-30 岁女性)。风险:麻疹 具有高度传染性,可能危及生命,尤其是 5 岁以下儿童。通过空气传播 传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生官员已经公布了这些病例,并强调了 疫苗接种 的重要性。对 暴发 的调查正在进行中,并可能正在进行接触者追踪。暴露源被确定为来自州外的旅行者。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Measles in Texas: 4 cases of measles confirmed in Montgomery County, health officials say - ABC13 Houston

News Brief
Montgomery County, Texas health officials have confirmed four cases of measles, involving four family members exposed to a traveler from out of state. One woman, aged 20-30, is hospitalized. None of the infected individuals had been vaccinated. Measles, a highly contagious viral infection, can lead to severe complications including encephalitis, particularly in young children. The virus spreads through airborne transmission, causing symptoms like fever, cough, runny nose, and rash. Health authorities are urging vaccination to prevent further spread and are conducting contact tracing to contain the outbreak.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Limited cooperation in some measles cases is making the virus harder to contain in Oregon - Oregon Public Broadcasting - OPB

News Brief
Oregon is facing challenges in containing the measles virus due to limited cooperation in some cases, according to Oregon Public Broadcasting (OPB). The report highlights that this cooperation issue is complicating efforts to control the spread of the virus in the state. While specific case numbers and detailed response measures are not provided, the article underscores the ongoing difficulty in managing measles outbreaks when affected individuals do not fully cooperate with health authorities. The situation is part of a broader trend, as the U.S. has already surpassed 2025's record for measles cases with five months left in 2026.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Delaware declares measles outbreak after fourth confirmed case of virus - Audacy

News Brief
Delaware has declared a measles outbreak following the confirmation of a fourth case of the virus, all involving unvaccinated men in Kent County. Health officials are actively conducting contact tracing to identify potential exposures. This outbreak occurs as Pennsylvania grapples with a separate, larger measles outbreak that has seen 120 cases since April, with 72 reported in Lancaster County alone. Measles, known for its high contagiousness, can lead to severe complications including pneumonia and brain inflammation. The MMR vaccine remains highly effective at preventing the disease, with a 97% efficacy rate.
Original source text (English)

Gretchen Whitmer endorses Haley Stevens in Democratic US Senate primary Jul 24, 2026 Business Virginia study on groundwater, data centers calls for tighter water regulations Jul 24, 2026 CloseGet the latest updates and insights delivered to your inboxI'm In!kywnewsradio>News> Delaware declares measles outbreak after fourth confirmed case of virus measlesdelaware CDC via Getty Images By KYW StaffJul 23, 2026Jul 23, 2026 06:23 AMKYW Staff See Full Bio Follow: LocalNewskywnewsradioPHILADELPHIA (KYW Newsradio) — The Delaware Division of Public Health has declared a measles outbreak after confirming the fourth case of the virus in the state.All of the infected individuals are unvaccinated men in Kent County.Health officials are doing contact tracing to determine if anyone else was exposed.The outbreak comes as Pennsylvania is dealing with its own measles outbreak. There have been 120 cases since April — 72 of them in Lancaster County.Measles is highly contagious. Symptoms prior to a rash include a cough, runny nose, and red, watery eyes. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

美国 麻疹 病例创 35 年来新高,连续第二年破纪录 - CNN

US measles cases reach 35-year high, for second record-breaking year in a row - CNN
新闻简报美国经历了严重的麻疹 暴发,病例数连续第二年创35年来新高。这种死灰复燃与该国自 2000 年以来消除该疾病的状况形成鲜明对比。公共卫生专家担心这对社区免疫力的影响以及进一步传播的可能性。暴发 强调了维持 疫苗接种 覆盖范围和解决 疫苗 犹豫问题所面临的持续挑战。卫生当局可能正在密切监视局势,但现有信息中尚未详细说明具体的应对措施。

时间
发布于 2026-07-24。证据中未指定事件日期。
地点与对象
实时更新 3 分钟前 与伊朗的战争 麻疹 环孢子虫病 麻疹 勒布朗·詹姆斯·乔·曼钦 特朗普的关税 CNN 强调 最佳空气净化器 • 突发新闻 • 突发新闻 Ethan Swope/Bloomberg/Getty Images 突发新闻 派拉蒙同意推迟华纳兄弟的上映。
事件
美国的 麻疹 病例达到 35 年来的新高,连续第二年打破纪录。2000年,这种疾病在美国被宣布消灭。
规模、影响与风险
病例数创35年来新高。没有提供具体的病例数、死亡数或暴露数。未明确说明风险级别。
应对、状态与不确定性
未提供有关调查、测试、隔离、接触者追踪、预防、治疗 或官方沟通的信息。来源限制:证据是联合摘要,而不是完整的原始文章。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

US measles cases reach 35-year high, for second record-breaking year in a row - CNN

News Brief
The United States has experienced a significant measles outbreak, with cases reaching a 35-year high for the second consecutive year. This resurgence marks a stark contrast to the disease's elimination status in the country since 2000. Public health experts are concerned about the implications for community immunity and the potential for further spread. The outbreak underscores ongoing challenges in maintaining vaccination coverage and addressing vaccine hesitancy. Health authorities are likely monitoring the situation closely, though specific response measures have not been detailed in the available information.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

There are already more US measles cases this year than in all of 2025 - AP News

News Brief
US health officials are investigating a new measles outbreak, with cases already surpassing the total for all of 2025. The outbreak highlights ongoing public health challenges, though specific case numbers and response measures remain unspecified. Authorities are urging vigilance as the situation develops, emphasizing the importance of vaccination to prevent further spread.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Two measles cases confirmed in Ashtabula County - Cleveland 19 News

News Brief
The Ashtabula County Health Department has confirmed two cases of measles in the county. The report, published on July 23, 2026, does not provide additional details on the affected individuals, potential exposures, or public health response measures. Measles is a highly contagious viral infection that can lead to severe complications, particularly in unvaccinated populations. Health officials typically respond to measles outbreaks with contact tracing, isolation of cases, and vaccination campaigns to prevent further spread. The confirmation of these cases underscores the importance of maintaining high vaccination coverage to protect community health.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

The measles outbreak is "slowing." A newborn's family still can't meet her. - CBS News

News Brief
The United States is experiencing a measles outbreak that has reached a 35-year high, though officials report the outbreak is slowing. The impact is deeply personal for one family, who cannot meet their newborn due to the outbreak. Doctors on the front lines are speaking out about the resurgence, highlighting the severity of the situation. While the outbreak is described as slowing, the high number of cases underscores ongoing public health challenges. The situation remains under investigation, with specific details about prevention measures and response efforts not yet fully reported.

Delaware declares measles outbreak after 4 confirmed cases - NBC10 Philadelphia

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Delaware declares measles outbreak after 4 confirmed cases - NBC10 Philadelphia

News Brief
Delaware has declared a measles outbreak following the confirmation of four cases, as reported by officials. The outbreak, declared on July 22, 2026, marks a public health concern in the state. While specific details about affected populations, transmission sources, or response measures are not provided in the evidence, the declaration underscores the importance of measles prevention and vaccination. The outbreak comes amid a broader trend of rising measles cases in 2026, which have reached a 35-year high according to recent reports. Health authorities are likely monitoring the situation closely to prevent further spread.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Measles cases hit another record: What families should know - Midland Reporter-Telegram

News Brief
The United States is experiencing a surge in measles cases, with current numbers significantly exceeding last year's pace and indicating a potential new record. The outbreak, reported by the Midland Reporter-Telegram, highlights the ongoing public health challenge posed by the highly contagious virus. While specific case counts and affected regions are not detailed in the available evidence, the upward trend underscores the importance of vaccination and awareness among families. Health authorities are likely monitoring the situation closely, though no official response measures or additional details have been disclosed in the provided information.
